"[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:7836]\n# K-WORK-007：多模型放置：共同容量、混合执行与租户公平\n\n多模型服务同时决定哪些版本驻留、各放在哪些设备、请求到达后去哪里，以及有限容量由哪些请求占有。完整权重不同的模型、共享基座的 adapters、同一模型的独立副本，具有不同的共享条件。模型名数量、逻辑资源份额和平均设备利用率都不能替代逐设备的容量与执行账。\n\n放置的输入包括模型及版本、可用执行配置、设备与链路、请求到达和长度轨迹、租户份额、数值要求与服务目标。输出包括逐设备物理对象、模型副本及执行组、冷对象加载规则、请求绑定与准入决定，以及不能满足的条件。离线规划选择长期布局，运行时按实际状态重新检查；预测可以改变选择顺序，不能放宽身份、容量和完成条件。\n\n## 先确定可共享的对象\n\n一个可执行模型身份至少包含基座及版本、adapter 完整对象、数值表示、目标层、分片与布局。adapter 对象包含每个目标矩阵的 A/B、有效因子内维 r、缩放 s 和对象版本。相同模型名、相同形状或相同 r 都不足以证明对象相同。\n\n采用列向量约定：\n\nu＝Ax，y＝W₀x＋sBu，\n\n其中 W₀∈R^{dₒ×dᵢ}、A∈R^{r×dᵢ}、B∈R^{dₒ×r}。r 是因子内维，rank(BA)≤r。固定 adapter 可形成 W′＝W₀＋sBA；运行时分支则在每个目标层共享基座矩阵乘，按请求加回各自增量，再把已加增量的隐藏状态传给下一层。不能先执行完整基座模型，最后才添加一次 adapter 修正。\n\n合并副本减少运行时低秩分支，但复制完整权重或支付串行切换成本。共享基座保留跨 adapter 合批机会，却增加分组、低秩计算、对象加载和版本管理。实数恒等式不保证浮点逐位一致；合并、量化与分组路径必须满足同一声明的误差和质量要求。r＝0 时直接跳过增量，不能计算 α/r。\n\n请求准入时固定绑定 β＝(请求生命期、租户、基座版本、adapter 版本、执行配置、状态身份)。路由别名更新只影响后续绑定。不同 adapter 下相同 token 可能产生不同 KV，即使 adapter 未直接修改 K/V 投影，上游隐藏状态也可能已经改变。默认把影响状态的 adapter 身份及版本纳入前缀身份；只有证明状态独立于某项依赖，才可省略。tokenizer、位置、模态内容和数值表示也需要相容性证明，不能由形状推断共享。\n\n[S-LORA](../sources/S-LORA.md) 给出低秩分支及合并关系；[S-SLORA](../sources/S-SLORA.md) 将共享基座计算与异 adapter 分支分开。[S-VLLM-PREFIX](../sources/S-VLLM-PREFIX.md) 的固定文档将父块哈希、块 token 和额外身份共同用于前缀检索，明确包含 LoRA ID 并只缓存完整块。允许同一 ID 更新权重时，还须补充版本或内容身份。哈希检索、授权与真实计算完成是不同条件。\n\n### 物理容量与逻辑份额\n\n对设备 d，令 U_d(t) 是时刻 t 实际占用或已承诺的唯一物理 backing 集合，容量账为\n\nM_d(t)＝Σ_{o∈U_d(t)} allocated_bytes(o)，max_t M_d(t)≤Cap_d。\n\n对象包括权重、adapter、KV、执行区、图缓冲、加载目的区、在途缓冲、待消费输出，以及分配器已保留但未使用的物理空间。已承诺的空闲页以后变成存活对象时只改变分类，不再加第二份容量。纯虚拟地址预留不计物理驻留；未 backing 的增长需求要作为未来分配承诺检查。\n\n容量键是设备与物理分配身份，不是内容哈希。同一基座 backing 被多个请求引用只计一次；同设备上两份内容相同但分配独立的副本仍计两次。同一物理键若出现不同大小或代次，必须先解决账本冲突，不能静默去重。\n\n上述不等式是总字节约束。即使字节足够，连续区间、对齐、页大小、可写权限、图地址、分片或内核布局也可能不满足。只有实际页映射及生命期也已确定，才能认定这个具体计划可分配。保守上界超预算说明当前预留方案不能被该上界证明可行，不证明其他分块、串行或重算方案都不可能。\n\n逻辑计费另设租户账。共享权重怎样分摊属于服务契约；不能把逻辑份额相加变成另一份物理权重，也不能用减少计费份额使活跃 KV 立即消失。借出的容量只有在请求结束、迁移、换出或重算路线实际释放后才可收回。\n\n## 驻留、并行与路由的候选\n\n独占副本便于归因和限制干扰，但低频模型可能长期闲置。完整模型共同驻留复用设备时间，却减少各模型的 KV 余量。冷模型按需加载以启动等待换容量，等待还包括旧版本排空、存储争用、转换、通信组建立与预热。共享基座的 LoRA 可只交换增量；极热且长期固定的 adapter 则可能适合合并副本。\n\n模型并行提供另一种复用方式。层间流水把不同层放到不同设备，让连续请求同时占据不同阶段；在各层执行效率不变的假设下，它不缩短孤立请求的串行计算，还增加阶段通信。收益来自突发期间的流水吞吐和可用容量。算子内并行可以缩短单请求计算，却增加集合通信和同步。实际分片必须给出逐 rank 权重、复制张量、KV、workspace 和链路账，不能用组内总显存代替逐设备约束。\n\n[AlpaServe v1](../sources/S-ALPASERVE.md) 研究模型并行与多模型统计复用，但主要评估一次前向的非自回归模型，不能把固定服务时间、batch 饱和或模拟器结果直接套到逐 token decode。执行组内一个设备故障可能破坏整个组；组内并行也不能替代不同故障域的独立副本。\n\n### 两设备流水为何可能获益，也可能反转\n\n以下是设定模型。模型 A/B 的完整权重各为 12 GiB；独占方案将 A 放设备 0、B 放设备 1。流水方案把两模型各分成两个 6 GiB 阶段：设备 0 保存 A₀/B₀，设备 1 保存 A₁/B₁，每设备权重仍为 12 GiB，执行区与请求状态另计。两个模型共享同一条 FIFO 流水，每设备一次执行一个阶段。\n\n设单设备一次请求耗时 D＝10 ms；流水每阶段耗时 5.5 ms，已包含这一设定的阶段开销。四条 A 请求同时到达、B 没有请求时，独占方案完成时刻为 10、20、30、40 ms，均值 25 ms。流水在设备 0 依次运行 A₀，设备 1 在对应前阶段完成后运行 A₁，完成时刻为 11、16.5、22、27.5 ms，均值 19.25 ms。首条更慢，突发均值却下降。\n\n若 A/B 同时各到达四条，独占方案的两设备同时工作，八条请求均值仍为 25 ms。共享流水的八个完成时刻从 11 ms 起每隔 5.5 ms 一个，均值变为 30.25 ms。两设备都持续饱和时，独占总服务率为 2/10 请求/ms，流水为 1/5.5 请求/ms，开销使后者更低。\n\n因此应保留模型间突发的时间相关性。只给每模型平均到达率，或分别打乱轨迹，会删除决定复用收益的同时突发关系。真实 decode 还包含长度、KV、批组成和逐轮公平变化，需重新构建事件与资源模型。\n\n## 放置与阶段划分怎样求解\n\n### 先生成合法执行配置\n\n设模型有 K 个保持拓扑顺序的层，候选流水阶段数为 S。对连续层段 [i,j)，预先给出阶段时间估计 ℓ(i,j) 和逐设备物理需求；不满足容量、布局或分片条件的层段标为不可行。在选择加性近似时，ℓ 可由逐层时间相加并加入相应通信；融合、cache 和不同并行配置改变时，必须重新校准，不能假设任意层段都可相加。\n\n令 F(s,k) 表示前 k 层分成 s 个非空阶段时，能够得到的最小最大阶段时间：\n\nF(0,0)＝0，其余未初始化状态为＋∞；\n\nF(s,k)＝min_{s−1≤i<k，段[i,k)可行} max(F(s−1,i),ℓ(i,k))。\n\n记录取得最小值的 i，从 F(S,K) 反向恢复所有阶段边界。不存在有限前驱即表示这个阶段数及配置下无解。空模型只接受零阶段；非空模型不允许零阶段或 S>K。时间复杂度为 O(SK²)，状态为 O(SK)，段成本准备另计。\n\n例如层时间为 [6,1,1,4] ms，分两阶段。按层数均分得到 [6,1] 与 [1,4]，瓶颈 7 ms；动态规划得到 [6] 与 [1,1,4]，瓶颈 6 ms。若后者第二阶段容量不够，必须删去该分割，不能保留时间最优却无法分配的配置。\n\n这个目标优化流水稳态间隔；孤立请求延迟还需加所有阶段及通信。阶段数增加也可能增加同时存活的激活、通信缓冲和状态版本，不能仅靠 F 值决定放置。\n\n### 有限候选上的放置搜索\n\n下面是从 AlpaServe 的配置枚举与模拟筛选思想推导的一种保守规划器，不声称复制其全部实现或找到全局最优。设备组在本候选族中互不重叠，同组允许驻留多个模型。其他共享组织可以另建候选。\n\n目标先明确：在固定 offered 轨迹上，最大化满足数值、TTFT、ITL/完成时间等共同约束的完成请求数；另检查每租户拒绝率和份额约束。成本向量保留设备时间、加载字节、容量峰值与等待；没有指定偏好时不把异单位量直接相加。\n\n```pseudocode\n输入：有限模型/版本集合；有限设备分组与执行配置；固定请求轨迹；\n      每设备容量、布局与链路条件；租户目标；束宽 B≥1；副本上限 R\n输出：满足声明约束的最佳已发现计划，或未找到及逐候选原因\n\n若模型集合为空，返回空计划；若配置集合为空，报告缺少候选\n对每个设备分组及配置组合：\n    用阶段划分和逐 rank 需求生成合法执行配置\n    初始化 beam 为仅含空放置，best 保存当前可接受计划\n    对深度 1 到 R：\n        extensions ← 空集合\n        对 beam 中每个计划 P：\n            对每个有限的 (模型版本，设备组，执行配置)：\n                构造增加一个副本的 P′，给独立 backing 新物理身份\n                共享只在已有兼容 backing 确实被共同引用时去重\n                建立权重、KV 承诺、workspace、加载与过渡版本生命期\n                若逐设备字节、映射、执行身份或链路硬条件失败，记录并跳过\n                按下述事件规则评估固定轨迹，保留所有拒绝与未完成请求\n                以完整计划签名去重，把 P′ 加入 extensions\n        若 extensions 为空，结束这个组合\n        按预先声明的目标排序并保留前 B 个进入 beam\n        仅当租户和服务约束满足时，更新 best；中间深度也可成为 best\n合并所有组合的 best；返回已发现结果和适用轨迹\n```\n\n束搜索丢弃的分支可能包含更好方案；有限候选未找到结果不等于所有放置都不可行。物理需求随驻留和共享集合变化时，要在每个扩展中重算，不能固定一个“每模型大小”做所有装箱。\n\n轨迹评估须展开执行依赖。对于固定 FIFO 流水，令 f(j,k) 是第 j 条请求在阶段 k 的完成时刻，阶段时间为 ℓ(j,k)：第一阶段等待请求到达及前一请求释放设备；后续阶段满足\n\nf(j,k)＝max(f(j,k−1),f(j−1,k))＋ℓ(j,k)。\n\n缺少的前驱视为初始设备空闲；加载和输入传输另作为必须完成的前驱。共享链路也有自己的占用时间，不能让两次传输各自使用独占带宽。真实模型有不同执行组或异构阶段时，使用对应设备和链路的事件依赖，不能继续套同一条 FIFO 递推。\n\n逐 token 服务还要在本轮完成、采样和状态提交之后产生下一轮候选。每个时刻先处理已完成的资源事件，再处理到达并运行准入/轮转；只有真正完成的对象才可释放或供消费者使用。拒绝不从 offered 分母删除，窗口结束仍未完成的请求单列。服务时间是估计时，结果属于该模型的有限回放，不能称为未来总体 SLO 保证。\n\n## 运行时准入、路由与公平\n\n### 可以等待加载，与可以提交执行不同\n\n设备组的基座、通信组和执行路径就绪后，某个冷 adapter 仍可尚未驻留。此时可以接受一个有完整资源承诺的待加载计划，但必须在实际加载完成后才能提交依赖它的执行。不能把“adapter 未 READY”一律当成永远不可路由，也不能把“允许等待加载”当成现在即可读取。\n\n准入状态包含未准入、已绑定待加载、就绪待服务、在途、待输出和终止；对象另记 FETCHING/READY、逻辑引用、实际读写者、发布资格和代次。这些责任可以同时存在，不能用单个枚举替代。\n\n```pseudocode\n输入：请求 r；实际设备与链路状态；对象目录；页池；租户账；输出预算\n输出：拒绝/等待原因，或不可变绑定及临时资源责任\n\n校验非空输入、长度与生成上限、权限、完整模型/adapter 对象和执行契约\n若无须生成且无需执行其他工作，按协议完成，不分配无用设备状态\n对每个基座与执行组已就绪的候选 g：\n    固定对象版本，取得已复用状态的真实计算边界\n    计算本轮新增可写 KV、共享尾页 COW、加载目的页、staging 和输出需求\n    只将回收条件已经满足的旧页算作当前可用空间\n    若缺少合法加载源、映射、容量、租户份额或执行路径，排除此计划\n    构造依赖时间线，估计排队、加载、计算、通信和输出的完成代价 E_g\n若合法计划为空，排队或拒绝，并记录具体阻塞原因\n选择 E_g 最小的合法计划；并列按预先声明的稳定规则处理\n在协调区内重新核对资源与代次，取得已有对象保护并预留全部新增资源\n任一步失败，撤销本请求尚未提交的临时改动，保留旧状态\n成功后发布请求绑定；冷对象登记复制责任并进入 FETCHING\n复制完成且完整对象、布局和分片核对成功，才发布 READY\n实际提交前再次确认全部依赖已 READY，登记读取、写入和输出责任\n完成后提交全层一致的新状态和允许的输出，再释放本次实际使用责任\n```\n\n已提交 DMA 或设备工作的目的页不能在失败/取消时立即归还；应停止新访问，排空已有操作后再回收。完整 KV 命中却没有末行 logits 时，仍需合法的末 token 重算或另有已验证的 logits 缓存；不能因为待计算长度为零就让请求永远停在就绪队列。具体 token 边界与安全写区由[连续批处理](../algorithms/iteration-batching.md)展开。\n\n设定路由候选 A 排队 100 ms、加载 0、计算 10；B 排队 10 ms、冷加载 40、计算 10，且能预留完整加载目的区；C 排队 0，但没有 adapter 目的空间。按串行设定，合法计划 A/B 的代价为 110/60 ms，选 B；B 要等加载完成才可执行，C 不能用最低队列绕过容量。\n\n若把 C 的队列改成 10，与 B 并列，忽略合法性的最短队列实现可能因输入顺序选 B，也可能选 C。因此该负对照不能断言“必选 C”。检查应保留这个并列回归，另用 C 严格最短的设定触发错误，并验证正确路由在候选重排后仍只选择合法最小代价。\n\n### 跨轮轮转与进度边界\n\n优先同 adapter 请求可减少活跃增量和准备开销，却可能使冷租户饥饿。一种明确的基线是维护持续存在的租户环和各租户 FIFO：新非空租户加入环尾，每次给环首一个合法最小服务片，然后仍有请求的租户移到环尾；一轮预算用尽后保留游标。未就绪或本轮容量不可行的请求保留阻塞记录，扫描其他可执行工作。\n\n```pseudocode\n初始化：租户环为空；每租户请求队列为空\n到达：请求进入本租户队尾；租户此前为空则加入环尾\n每轮：\n    初始化本轮 token、页、时间与输出预算\n    按持久环顺序访问租户\n    为其队首或契约允许的首个就绪请求尝试一个最小服务片\n    若可行，完成预留并记入不可变执行计划，扣减相应预算\n    若失败，撤销这次临时预留，记录原因；不得消耗虚假的服务额度\n    仍非空的租户移到环尾；保留跨轮位置\n    预算耗尽，或完整一圈没有任何可行进展时结束本轮\n完成事件：按请求和代次提交进度，修正实际服务成本，移除已终止请求\n```\n\n若持续非空且就绪的租户至多 M 个，每个最小片资源可行，资源最终完成，新租户不插到既有等待者前面，则一个这样的租户至多经过 M 个成功服务机会取得一次进展。若每个机会到下一次调度的时间不超过 T，可得到 M·T 的粗等待上界。它保证租户进度，不自动保证租户队内每条请求同样的界；还需有限前驱和有限准入。\n\n每轮重新按租户名从头扫描不是上述轮转。M＝3、每轮仅一个片且 A/B/C 一直就绪时，持久轮转为 A、B、C、A……；每轮重置为 A 优先会使 B/C 无进展。无限输出背压、不可回收页或不可完成设备操作同样会破坏进度前提，必须限准入或使用有证明的恢复路线。\n\n公平单位也要明确。等 token 不等于等设备时间：相同 query 数在历史 32 与 32768 时有不同 attention 工作。可以按预测设备时间扣欠额，再以实际观测修正，但要限制一次不可抢占片，并保留硬容量检查。\n\n## 共享基座场景的完整成本账\n\n以下均为设计设定，适用于声明的稠密 decoder 和状态表示，不是 GPU 测量。单设备可用 24 GiB，基座 12 GiB，workspace/执行缓存 3 GiB，额外保留 1 GiB，adapter 与 KV 的共同预算为 8 GiB。额外保留与其中实际对象必须避免重复计数。\n\n共有 128 个 adapter，32 层、每层两个 4096×4096 线性层使用 rank 16、FP16 LoRA。一个目标矩阵的增量为\n\nr(dᵢ＋dₒ)b_w＝16×8192×2＝262144 字节＝256 KiB。\n\n64 个目标矩阵使一个 adapter 为 16 MiB，128 个共 2 GiB 主存；主存中的冷对象还与 KV 换出、加载转换和分词竞争。16 条请求使用不同 adapter 时，端侧增量为 256 MiB。同一 adapter 被多请求使用时，只有真实共享 backing 才去重。\n\n普通稠密 GQA 的全层 KV 主项为\n\nb_KV＝2LH_kv d_h b_s 字节/token，\n\nL 为层数，H_kv 为 KV head 数，d_h 为 head 维数，b_s 为状态元素字节。不同 K/V 维数、量化、MLA、滑窗或其他状态表示须重写此式。设本例 b_KV＝128 KiB/token，每条 2048-token 历史需要 256 MiB；16 条共 4 GiB，加 adapter 为 4.25 GiB\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 12000:13608]\nm 执行在该版本结论中属于未来工作。本文的公平基线和生命周期是独立推导。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-SLORA/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-SLORA/fulltext.txt)\n\n- [S-LORA：LoRA: Low-Rank Adaptation of Large Language Models](../sources/S-LORA.md)：固定 2106.09685v2。§4.1 给出 W₀x＋BAx、α/r 缩放与合并构造；§4.2 说明吸收 A/B 后不便在同一 forward 中批量处理不同任务。本文据此区分固定合并副本与共享基座运行时分支，推导增量参数量；数值契约、版本并存和容量例为本库分析，不是论文训练质量或设备延迟结论。\n "0a145b68a31b03280284b09fa7a98e246c7cae7e92868d00ce524ea9fe759679", "reading_scope": "excerpt", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:7235]\n# K-WORK-008：弹性服务：启动延迟、可用容量与排空\n\n弹性服务把负载变化转成启动、开放、排空和回收决定。控制面期望的副本数、已分配设备数、已启动进程数、完成预热的执行组数，以及能够接收新请求的容量，属于不同状态。新增容量只有在执行组验证合格且准入路由提交后才存在；缩容也只有在最后使用责任结束后才释放物理资源。\n\n本章采用一个保守控制模型：路由、准入门和请求责任登记在共同权威状态域中排序，设备执行异步推进。该选择说明必须满足的原子关系，不要求所有工作使用同一把锁，也不声称所引论文已经实现本章协议。\n\n## 从副本数到可兑现容量\n\n设时间 t 的单位为秒。不可变制品版本为 v；一次准备尝试为 g＝(槽位, 不复用代次)；执行计划 P 包含完整 rank、实际设备和布局；公开请求范围为 Ω_g。就绪证书 C_g 将 v、g、P、环境依赖、范围与验证证据绑定在一起。相同版本的失败重试也使用新的 g，不能只按容器名或槽位接收完成通知。\n\nq_g 是在声明请求类、负载区间、资源预算和质量合同下的合格服务率，不是 GPU 峰值。混合请求应保留容量向量。LLM 至少区分 prefill、decode、KV 增长与输出背压；队列中的请求数或 token 数不能直接替代设备秒。\n\n| 状态 | 新请求准入 | 保留的责任 | 新准入容量 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| PREPARING | 关闭 | 预留、加载、转换、建组、编译与预热 | 0 |\n| READY | 关闭 | 已验证执行组，等待显式路由提交 | 0 |\n| OPEN | 按当前路由和证书检查 | 已接受请求及实际访问 | 条件化 q_g |\n| DRAINING | 关闭 | 已绑定请求可继续，已有访问与输出仍受保护 | 0 |\n| RETIRING | 关闭 | 拆除绑定、结算最后访问和输出借用 | 0 |\n| RETIRED | 关闭 | 实例资源已经归还 | 0 |\n| FAILED | 关闭 | 保留失败前已经产生的未结责任 | 0 |\n\n对一个路由范围，权威记录为 A＝(e,T)，e 是递增路由代次，T 是可供新准入选择的实例身份集合。正常提交保持 OPEN 的准入目标集合与 T 一致；若某个 OPEN 的证书失效，立即禁止其新准入并撤出 T，不能继续兑现历史容量。\n\n令 A_g(t)＝1 表示 g∈T、状态为 OPEN 且 C_g 当前有效，否则为 0，则\n\nμ_new(t)＝Σ_g A_g(t)q_g(t)。\n\nDRAINING 可以继续完成已绑定请求，但不能再次作为新请求容量。已有请求、加载和排空还会争用 OPEN 使用的物理资源，因此 q_g 必须是在这些占用之后能够兑现的预算。不能为同一块设备的 OPEN 和 DRAINING 各分配一份独占吞吐。\n\n对资源 r，令 a_{g,r} 为每单位服务消耗的资源量，R_r 为可用资源率，H_r 为突发、故障和切换余量，则候选服务率需满足\n\nΣ_g a_{g,r}(t)q_g(t)＋L_r(t)≤R_r(t)−H_r(t)，\n\nL_r 是加载、排空等未包含在 q_g 中的占用，不能重复计入。CPU、存储出口、PCIe、互连和设备执行分别检查。只有所有阶段按固定比例消耗同一资源向量时，才可用逐资源上界的最小值简化；一般情况需要阶段队列和事件模型。\n\n## 启动路径与可用时间\n\n启动是依赖图：取得资源和配额；固定制品清单；读取分片；建立主机页或固定块池；转换、对齐与校验；H2D；建立完整执行组和通信上下文；建立或核对编译产物、图与工作区；隔离预热；共同验证；最后提交路由。\n\n对事件 j，前驱集合为 pred(j)，没有额外资源争用时，最早完成时刻为\n\nF_j＝max_{i∈pred(j)}F_i＋t_j。\n\n无前驱事件以允许开始时刻为边界。共享 SSD、CPU、NIC 或 PCIe 的任务还要加入资源次序或带宽约束，不能同时给每个任务独占带宽并假定完全重叠。\n\n若 rank r 的本地排队为 Q_r，本地准备为 D_r，之后共同验证为 T_group，则\n\nT_ready＝max_r(Q_r＋D_r)＋T_group。\n\n已经包含在 D_r 中的工作不能再加进 T_group。两个 rank 分别排队 0、1 s，本地各需 4 s，后续共同验证 0.5 s，则为 5.5 s，不能串行相加成 9.5 s。路由安装及必要确认另计：T_available＝T_ready＋T_route_check。安全拒绝过期路由保证身份，但拒绝期间不产生可兑现吞吐。\n\n[ServerlessLLM](../sources/S-SERVERLESSLLM.md) §4 将每 GPU 张量组成顺序数据文件，用名称到 GPU、偏移和大小的索引恢复地址；主机固定块池和多阶段队列串联存储、主存与设备复制。其 §6.2 使用 q＋n/b 估计加载，其中 q 为服务器加载队列等待，n 为模型或分片字节，b 为所用流水的慢层有效带宽。该估计依赖每服务器顺序加载及反馈校准，不覆盖任意并发启动、转换、编译、预热和分布式组装，也不能将热缓存带宽用于冷启动。\n\n数据流必须落到实际对象：存储字节进入主机缓冲，CPU 转换读写目的区，设备复制读取源区并写入 HBM，tensor 索引和执行对象引用这些地址，验证消费完成后的对象，路由提交才开放新准入。源、目的和地址绑定都必须保留到其最后使用结束。\n\n## 合法容量、准入与资源账\n\n部分 rank 完成、通信组未建立、图只完成一半或代表路径失败时，实例不能成为 READY，更不能成为 OPEN。对设备 d，令 U_d(t) 为同一时刻占用预算的唯一物理 backing 集合，必须满足\n\nM_d(t)＝Σ_{o∈U_d(t)}size(o)，max_t M_d(t)≤Cap_d。\n\n集合包括权重、adapter、KV、工作区、图池、转换与 staging、通信区、预热状态、加载目的区及未消费输出。完整池已覆盖其内部活跃对象时不能再加一次；同一物理 backing 被多个读者引用只计一次，同设备上两份独立复制仍计两次。主机、设备和存储分别建账。总字节合格还不能代替对齐、连续区间、布局和映射可行性。\n\n一次准入需在权威域重新检查\n\nAdmit(r,g)＝g∈T ∧ state(g)＝OPEN ∧ C_g.valid ∧ σ_r∈Ω_g ∧ e_request＝e ∧ reserve(r,g)可取得。\n\nσ_r 包含输入解释、长度、dtype、adapter 和状态范围。预留覆盖承诺的在途增长，不能只看当前 KV 字节。取得预留、登记逻辑责任和写入不可变绑定 (r,v,g) 必须共同提交；失败返回等待或拒绝，不建立半份正式请求。请求身份重试返回原决定，输入摘要冲突则拒绝。\n\n准入先于路由提交时，即使请求消息之后才抵达 worker，也仍由原 g 履行；路由提交先发生时，旧 e 的新准入须被拒绝或重新解析。缓存路由只能提出候选，不能绕过权威门签发票据。分散租约可以替代共同状态域，但必须另证撤销、租约存活与排空的关系，不能用估计传播时间代替。\n\n## 控制算法与三种路由提交\n\n控制输入包括 offered 轨迹、各阶段剩余工作、当前实例与责任集合、逐资源账、启动估计、故障余量及策略参数。输出包括启动票据、准入决定、路由提交、排空与回收，以及每项未结责任。阈值、滞回、最短驻留和候选排序是策略；身份、完整执行组、资源约束和最后使用顺序是硬条件。\n\n```pseudocode\n算法 ElasticTick(now, events, policy)\n1. 按实例身份、操作身份和受保护范围幂等处理真实完成事件。\n   旧代次、未知操作或冲突载荷不能结算当前责任。\n   READY 通知只按下述准备完成协议处理，不能直接覆盖状态。\n2. 核对证书和实际依赖；失效实例关闭新准入并撤出权威目标集合。\n   从当前 T、OPEN 状态及有效证书重算新准入容量和逐资源预算。\n3. 按请求类和阶段估计剩余工作；保留 offered、拒绝及未完成请求。\n   用当前容量和预计启动依赖推演积压与 deadline 风险。\n   尚在准备或 READY 的容量仅进入预测，不能签发即时准入。\n4. 需要启动且策略允许时，枚举逐 rank 资源、制品位置、执行计划、\n   故障域及共享链路；先排除非法候选，再比较 T_available 和边际容量。\n5. 取得完整执行组的预留后，创建新 g 和唯一 prepare_op，置 PREPARING。\n   登记准备依赖及完成条件，关闭准入；预留失败则等待、换候选或拒绝。\n6. 准备达到全部条件后进入 READY。根据意图分别提交 FIRST、APPEND\n   或 REPLACE；READY 通知本身不执行这些路由操作。\n7. 缩容使用 STOP，只移除明确选中的目标；重新检查最短驻留、\n   故障后余量及排空预算，不把预计释放的资源提前借出。\n8. 对 DRAINING 且 R 为空的实例，封闭全部新业务与绑定产生入口，\n   进入 RETIRING；按依赖推进绑定拆除和 U/O 完成结算。\n   R、U、B、O 全空且拆除完成后才归还资源、进入 RETIRED。\n9. 准备或执行失败时撤销新准入，保存最早失败和未结责任；\n   停止未来业务，按恢复协议结束 R，再走相同拆除与结算路径。\n   新尝试使用新 g；超时不能充当设备停止或资源释放证据。\n10. 若有合法等待请求却无可执行目标，明确选择有后继事件的等待、\n    合同允许的降级或拒绝；记录无资源、无候选或失去完成证据的原因。\n```\n\n准备过程展开如下。\n\n```pseudocode\n算法 PrepareReplica(v, g, prepare_op, P, Ω)\n1. 固定不可变清单与依赖；核对分片、索引、形状、长度、dtype、\n   processor、adapter 和量化/布局元数据。\n2. 按主机与每张设备求准备期唯一 backing 并集与峰值，计入旧对象、\n   staging、图池、通信区和隔离预热状态；取得全组预留。\n3. 按块读取并转换到受控缓冲；异步读写提交前登记源与目的使用票据。\n4. 完成设备复制和 tensor 地址映射，建立相容 rank 集合与通信上下文。\n5. 按真实依赖核对编译缓存、图和工作区，不能仅用模型名判断命中。\n6. 在实际执行线程运行代表路径、边界和必要独立参考比较，\n   核对全部必要 rank 的共同结果；预热状态不得混入正式请求。\n7. 等待影响执行可读性的完成证据，清理或隔离预热状态。\n   汇总同一 (v,g,prepare_op,P,Ω) 的全部必要证据后发送完成通知。\n8. 任一步失败则停止后继准备、记录最早失败、排空已提交访问，\n   解除可解除绑定并释放未使用预留；不能凭部分成功发布 READY。\n```\n\nREADY 通知的允许源状态只有 PREPARING。接收端核对当前 g、登记的 prepare_op、证书身份与范围、全 rank 完成和依赖有效性，全部满足才执行 PREPARING→READY，并保存已接受通知的指纹。同身份同载荷的重复通知返回原处理结果，状态不变；即使实例已经 OPEN、DRAINING、RETIRING 或 RETIRED，也不能重新写成 READY。相同操作身份配不同载荷是冲突；同槽旧代次、未知操作、准备已经撤销后的首次通知均无状态效果。证书失效后到达的历史重复通知也不能恢复其有效性；重新准备使用新 g。\n\n路由操作另有唯一 op 和不可变参数。设新增集合为 H，明确移除集合为 D；操作在同一权威排序点执行。\n\n```pseudocode\n算法 CommitRoute(op, expected_e, mode, H, D)\n1. 若 op 已有提交记录：参数一致则返回原决定，不再变更状态或代次；\n   参数冲突则拒绝。确认丢失时查询该记录，不能按超时反向切换。\n2. 要求当前 e=expected_e；H、D 身份完整，互不相交。\n   H 中每个实例必须为 READY、证书有效、资源仍已取得，\n   且符合本次公开范围与版本变更授权；H 不得已经属于 T。\n3. 按 mode 验证并构造目标集合：\n   FIRST：要求 T 为空、H 非空、D 为空，T′←H。\n   APPEND：要求 T 非空、H 非空、D 为空，T′←T∪H。\n   REPLACE：要求 H 非空、D 非空且 D⊆T，T′←(T−D)∪H。\n   STOP：要求 H 为空、D 非空且 D⊆T，T′←T−D。\n   任一前提失败，整个操作无部分更新。\n4. 在同一提交中：仅关闭 D 的新准入并置 DRAINING；\n   打开 H 的准入并置 OPEN；保留 T−D 的状态与准入；\n   写入 A←(e+1,T′) 和 op 的已提交决定。\n5. 不改变任何已接受请求的 (r,v,g)、预留或实际访问责任。\n```\n\nAPPEND 没有隐含的旧目标。两台 OPEN 追加第三台时必须保留原两台；REPLACE 才关闭指定旧目标。FIRST 处理从零容量启动，也可用于当前目标集合已经为空但旧实例仍在排空的情形，前提仍需满足并存资源账。替换部分目标可以保留其他 OPEN，滚动更新由多个显式提交组成；它不是一次跨集群的天然原子事务。\n\n本章不通过 READY 通知重新开放 DRAINING。若需要复用尚未拆除的旧实例，应定义独立重新验证与发布操作；已经 RETIRING 的实例必须重新准备，不能仅改回状态标签。\n\n## 无循环等待的排空与退役\n\n为每个 g 记录四类责任：R_g 是仍可继续执行的逻辑请求；U_g 是 kernel、复制、传输及必要拆除操作的实际访问票据；B_g 是图、注册区与执行对象的地址绑定；O_g 是仍借用实例资源的输出消费者。每张票据记录实例、操作身份和受保护范围。业务取消只关闭未来业务，不能伪造 U/O 完成。\n\nDRAINING 关闭新请求和无关联后台业务入口，但已有 R 可以按原票据继续迭代、登记必要 U，以及在已授权范围中建立所需绑定。否则“停止新准入”会错误中断旧请求。所有这些登记必须与请求结束及进入 RETIRING 的决定排序。\n\n```pseudocode\n算法 RetireReplica(g)\n1. 关闭新准入并从 T 移除 g；保留全部已绑定请求和资源，置 DRAINING。\n2. 对每个请求的结束、取消或合法迁移：先关闭其未来业务提交入口，\n   再结算对应 R；已经登记的 U、B、O 继续保留。\n3. 在权威域确认 g 不在 T、准入门关闭、R 为空。\n   原子封闭准备、后台、图重放及其他新业务和新绑定入口，进入 RETIRING。\n   这里允许 U、B、O 非空，不等待 B 先消失。\n4. 根据每个绑定的最后使用依赖推进拆除：\n   图等待已登记重放结束；注册区等待相关 DMA 与借用者结束。\n   依赖完成后解除该绑定，结算对应 B。\n   拆除若产生异步访问，必须先登记独立 U 票据；只允许有限的已授权\n   拆除任务产生这种访问，不恢复业务入口或新增任意绑定。\n5. 持续按真实证据结算 U 和 O，重复通知不重复扣减。\n   U 的完成不能以“B 已经全部拆除”为前提，绑定拆除也不能等待资源归还；\n   按最后使用依赖建立有向无环的后继关系。\n6. 仅当 R、U、B、O 全空、拆除任务已完成且没有任何产生新责任的入口，\n   销毁剩余执行对象并归还资源一次，进入 RETIRED。\n7. 无法证明完成时保留隔离资源，列出未结票据与所需证据；\n   排空期限触发终止/恢复策略，不能直接跳到 RETIRED。\n```\n\n一条完整组合轨迹可以检查推进性。旧 g 已移出路由；请求 r 被取消后 R 为空，但仍有 kernel k、输出 DMA d、图绑定 graph、注册绑定 reg 和输出借用 o。graph 的最后使用依赖 k；o 的交接依赖 d；reg 的解除依赖 d 和 o。\n\n| 事件 | 状态与责任 | 后继 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 撤销准入，r 尚活跃 | DRAINING，R={r} | r 仍可按旧票据执行 |\n| r 取消并关闭未来提交 | R=∅，U={k,d}，B={graph,reg}，O={o} | 封闭入口，立即可进入 RETIRING |\n| 尝试提前拆 graph 或归还资源 | k 未完成，绑定和访问仍存活 | 拒绝，保留 backing |\n| d 完成 | U={k}，O={o} | 可以完成输出交接，不能仅据 DMA 完成删除 O |\n| o 完成交接，解除 reg | O=∅，B={graph} | k 与 graph 继续保护旧资源 |\n| k 完成，解除 graph | R、U、B、O 全空 | 拆除结束后归还资源并 RETIRED |\n| 旧 READY 或重复完成迟到 | 已保存旧决定与票据结算 | 无状态倒退、无第二次归还 |\n\nk 与 d 可以并行，表中的先后是一种合法交错。若逻辑请求结束时要求 B 已为空才进入 RETIRING，而唯一拆 B 的动作又只在 RETIRING 中运行，就会永久停滞。若忽略 B，只凭 R/\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 12000:14209]\ntch13/K-WORK-008/round-2/check-0.json)。\n\n所有检查均为串行事件与理想完成证据的 CPU 逻辑模型，不是 GPU、PCIe、NVMe、网络、图销毁或注册解除的实测，也不证明真实数值结果、尾延迟、SLO 或知识改善了 Agent 构建效果。目标系统仍需验证权威排序、实际票据覆盖、设备最后使用证据、共享干扰、控制崩溃恢复及长流排空。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-SERVERLESSLLM：ServerlessLLM: Locality-Enhanced Serverless Inference for Large Language "knowledge/system-semantics/tenant-isolation.md", "sha256": "0ca7501a80e750152a5fb3b9815c77f9e3f9aa393ff7546b3f05375958f88606", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-SYS-016：租户隔离：资源份额、缓存命名空间与故障边界\n\n一个推理租户可以同时消耗 CPU、设备计算、KV 容量、adapter 驻留、网络、I/O 与响应缓冲。隔离设计应分别说明谁能够访问哪些对象、谁最多占用多少资源、谁在竞争时优先推进，以及一个故障影响哪些请求。限制其中一项不会自动建立其他保证。\n\n## 从入口身份传到状态拥有者\n\n入口确定的租户、模型/adapter 版本和请求身份，需要传递到调度、缓存、传输与输出。不同租户即使提交相同文本，也不默认取得对方的 KV、检索内容、adapter 或 trace。共享不可变公共前缀应以明确的公共对象契约和版本身份为前提，而不是通过发现相同 hash 就绕过授权。\n\n请求 ID 本身不是访问凭证。对共享缓存，授权范围与数值兼容性同时满足才允许命中；页句柄、远端地址和日志引用也不能只依赖可猜的槽号。过期版本退出目录后，在途访问仍要结束，最后回收时清除对新拥有者不应暴露的内容或重新初始化全部可读范围。\n\ntrace 保存排队、形状、长度、代次与资源事件通常已足以做归因。确需记录原始输入或输出时，把存取范围、保留时间和删除责任纳入同一对象生命周期。租户隔离不是要求所有状态复制一份，而是要求共享的条件和可见范围可验证。\n\n## 主机限额的实际含义\n\n[Linux 6.12 cgroup v2](../sources/S-LINUX-RESOURCES.md) 区分权重、上限、保护与独占分配。CPU 权重决定竞争时的相对份额，CPU 带宽上限约束一段时间可消耗的执行时间，cpuset 约束可运行的 CPU/内存节点；它们不能互相替代。CPU 配额不保证独占 cache、内存带宽或确定的尾延迟。\n\nmemory.high 会引入回收压力和节流，memory.max 在无法回收时可触发 cgroup 内 OOM；文件页缓存、匿名内存与部分内核/网络缓冲也可能计入预算。只统计模型 tensor 会遗漏这些消耗。内存控制器不是所有 GPU 显存对象的自动配额器，GPU 容量仍需执行器和实际设备机制约束。RDMA 对象数、线程/进程数量与存储流量也可能先于字节预算耗尽。\n\n## GPU 共享必须绑定具体机制\n\n下列界限取自固定的 [NVIDIA device plugin v0.16.1](../sources/S-NVIDIA-PLUGIN.md) README。这里说明该版本公开的资源与部署契约，不把插件文档扩展成所有设备、驱动或攻击场景的安全证明。\n\n| 机制 | 该来源明确支持的理解 | 不能由资源名称推出的保证 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 整卡分配 | 将指定设备暴露给容器，区分 UUID/index 等身份传递方式 | 其他宿主进程是否仍可访问、整机故障是否隔离 |\n| CUDA time-slicing | 多个访问份额指向同一底层 GPU；文档明确没有额外的显存与故障域隔离 | 每份额对应固定显存或固定吞吐；申请更多份额按比例得到算力 |\n| 插件的 MPS 共享 | 控制进程按份额限制客户端可用显存与计算能力；该版本只支持完整 GPU，并不支持启用 MIG 的设备 | 与独占物理设备相同的尾延迟、性能无干扰或覆盖所有错误的隔离 |\n| MIG 资源暴露 | 在硬件支持前提下，none/single/mixed 策略决定暴露方式；mixed 可暴露按 profile 命名的资源 | 任意 profile 组合均合法、profile 吞吐线性缩放，或共享宿主/网络/供电故障自动消失 |\n\nMIG、MPS 与 time-slicing 不应被合并成一个“虚拟 GPU”容量数字。MIG profile 需要对应实际硬件支持与配置，多个访问份额也可能继续共享同一 MIG 资源。更细的硬件隔离承诺需要相应固定硬件文档和故障测试；本章不从插件的资源暴露推断未记录的保证。\n\n## 容量、公平与故障预算\n\n设定共享设备的 KV 池为 8 GiB，租户 A/B 各保留 3 GiB，另有 2 GiB 可借用池。借用可在空闲时增加利用率，但 A 已有活跃请求占用的借用状态，不能在 B 到来时立即按账面扣回；必须有合法结束、迁移、重算或拒绝路线。仅设“每租户 10 请求”的限额，会让长上下文租户占据远多于短请求租户的容量。\n\n再设一张卡被 time-slicing 暴露为 8 份。接收 8 个各需要 10 GiB 的服务进程，不会把物理显存变成 80 GiB。共享份额数量只允许更多访问者，模型加载和峰值容量仍需真实字节账。同样，空闲时一个租户借到更多设备时间，不代表竞争时能继续获得相同服务率。\n\n## 验证与选择改变\n\n构造两租户相同输入但不同权限/adapter 的请求，检查缓存、结果、日志与迟到消息无越界归属；再让一个租户持续增长 KV、生成超长响应、启动大量冷 adapter、消耗 CPU 配额与存储流量。观察另一个租户的准入、尾延迟和可完成工作量，不能只检查进程没有退出。\n\n故障测试按已承诺的边界设计：终止一个 worker、制造配额内存不足、使一个共享设备不可用或让网络中断，确认影响范围、可重试语义和清理责任。对全卡故障、宿主故障与租户进程错误分别记录，不能把某一种测试通过推广到全部错误。\n\n严格隔离需求可能要求独占设备或独立执行域；低占用且允许干扰的服务可采用共享。统一基座内的多租户执行节省内存，却把应用调度器变成主要容量和身份边界，必须同时有明确的资源账与独立污染测试。\n\n<!-- quota/权重、内存压力/上限、cpuset、进程和设备/RDMA 控制；完整 powercap 文档定义计量域、能量单位/范围和功率约束窗口。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-LINUX-RESOURCES/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-LINUX-RESOURCES/fulltext.txt)\n\n- "selected_characters": 4131, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "knowledge/system-semantics/storage-services.md", "sha256": "283ceab23fbbf5c27bbb4486b56c2123459a19a00229c5baae70d09c18062661", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-SYS-018：推理存储服务：制品、缓存、索引与提交记录\n\n推理存储既包含权重等稳定制品，也包含可重算 KV、检索索引和不可随意丢弃的提交记录。它们要求的持久性、访问粒度、更新方式与故障处理不同。把所有对象放入同一个高速缓存，或者全部按最强持久化处理，都可能付出不必要的代价。\n\n## 先定义对象契约\n\n| 对象 | 主要读取/更新模式 | 丢失后的恢复条件 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 模型、adapter、tokenizer 与量化制品 | 版本内只读，分片/范围读取，批量分发 | 从可信且完整的相同版本重新取得；缺片不能继续发布 |\n| 可复用 KV 或中间状态 | 按前缀/层/块读取，逐步产生或封闭 | 只有保留完整输入、模型身份和恢复语义时才可重算 |\n| embedding 与检索索引 | 查询、批量构建、增量更新或整体替换 | 需要原始文档、embedding 版本、索引参数与删除/更新记录 |\n| 请求提交、计费或必须保留的事件 | 追加和查询，通常小而频繁 | 能否丢失与重放由协议决定；不能由“模型可重算”推断 |\n| 临时 staging、可再生编译缓存 | 短期读写，格式与环境绑定 | 可删除重建，但重建成本和峰值必须进入启动预算 |\n\n可重算只描述恢复路线，不等于恢复免费。缓存丢失后集中重算会争用 CPU/GPU，影响仍正常的请求；索引重建还可能包含长时间的数据扫描。某些递归状态、工具结果和随机过程无法仅凭文本恢复，必须明确保留范围。\n\n## 对象、文件与块的接口选择\n\n对象接口用键与版本定位整体对象或范围，适合不可变分片和分发，但不能假设跨多个对象的原子发布或目录 rename 语义。文件接口提供路径、偏移与文件系统操作，具体原子性、缓存可见性和持久性仍依赖所选实现。块接口提供地址空间与 I/O，文件布局、版本和发布由上层负责。\n\n这些接口不是天然的快慢顺序。大量小元数据请求可能限制启动，大对象顺序读则可能受出口带宽限制。范围读取是否真正减少下游字节、直接 I/O 的对齐与并发条件、mmap 是否产生缺页或双重缓存，都要接回[存储 I/O](../hardware/storage-io.md)与[加载布局](../frameworks/checkpoint-loading.md)。\n\n[Ceph 2006](../sources/S-CEPH06.md) 展示数据与元数据路径分离、计算式放置、跨故障域副本以及可见确认和落盘提交的区别。它是机制来源；当时原型的安全协议和 MDS 恢复尚未完整实现，不能据此承诺当前后端的 API、可靠性或性能。\n\n## 完整版本如何发布与读取\n\n写入者先产生不可变分片，再检查完整集合的大小、表示和必要内容校验，最后发布能引用全部已完成对象的版本清单。读者先取得一个确定的清单版本，再按该版本读取所有分片；不能在每次读取时重新解析可能变化的 latest 指针，从而把新旧权重拼在一起。\n\n发布指针的原子性需要所选存储提供明确操作，或由外部权威记录维护；没有跨对象事务时，不应假装多次上传天然原子。部分写入失败留在未发布集合，重试不会让半成品进入可读版本。回退改动新请求的版本选择，旧读者仍须能够完成；删除旧版本应等待实际读者和保留策略允许。\n\n数据已到客户端内存、已被多个副本接收、可供其他客户端读取、已达到所需持久性，是不同完成点。对请求提交日志尤其要说明何时可以对外承诺成功；对可重算缓存则可以选择较弱持久性并承受 miss，但有效性和租户身份检查不能因此省掉。\n\n## 分发、复制与恢复的共同带宽\n\n设定一个制品 32 GiB，16 个节点同时从共享出口获取，出口有效读带宽为 8 GiB/s。没有本地命中或对等分发时，总出口需要交付 512 GiB，最后全部取得的纯传输下界为 64 s，不能为每节点都使用 4 s 的独占估计。若每机已有一份相同版本的本地缓存，出口流量可减少，但本地读、校验、转换与 H2D 仍在关键路径。\n\n树形或对等分发减少集中出口压力，代价是节点间链路、临时存储和额外失败路径；收到的内容仍须属于同一清单版本。缓存可以合并并发 miss，避免相同分片被重复拉取，但取消一个等待者不能误删其他等待者还需要的共享下载。\n\n容量账要区分逻辑数据与副本。设逻辑制品 100 GiB、三副本，则数据主项已为 300 GiB，尚未包含元数据、旧版本、恢复过程的新副本与临时写入。恢复和重平衡还会消费在线读取所需的磁盘和网络；后端报告“可恢复”不等于恢复期间仍满足原来的启动或 KV 延迟。\n\n## 故障验证与选择反转\n\n测试缺片、截断、错误版本、过期目录、部分范围完成、读过程中版本切换、旧版本清理、共享下载取消、节点失效和恢复流量并发。成功条件是读者只得到完整且相容的对象，失败可以定位到阶段，原有有效副本的责任没有提前消失。\n\n性能分别测元数据操作、冷读/热读、范围读取、并发启动与恢复期尾延迟。权重长期稳定且节点重复启动时，本地缓存更有价值；模型版本频繁变化或节点短命时，共享源与分发效率更关键。对 KV，比较远端恢复、压缩和重算的完整费用；对不可丢的提交状态，则先满足协议的持久性要求，再优化路径。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-CEPH06：Ceph: A Scalable, High-Performance Distributed File System](../sources/S-CEPH06.md)：§2–4 数据/元数据分离与目录分布；§5 计算式对象放置、故障域、副本、可见 ack/落盘 commit、失败检测与恢复；§6–9 原型评估和未完成范围。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-CEPH06/reader.html) · "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "knowledge/system-semantics/deployment-environment.md", "sha256": "1b47e39a7f36c9f1cba4dc1792ad9243fd69d083ae1d6a7bb13bfd80dae1a8a5", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-SYS-019：部署环境：版本契约、可见设备与真实资源边界\n\n部署描述必须能够解释模型实际运行在哪些资源上。镜像、宿主内核/驱动、设备暴露、用户态库、CPU/内存限制、文件与网络路径共同决定可执行性和性能。容器能够启动，不代表这些条件已经形成有效的推理环境。\n\n## 环境身份与相容关系\n\n记录模型制品、执行器和编译器版本，用户态计算/通信库，宿主驱动与内核，以及实际设备能力。兼容关系按目标平台的固定文档和验证确定，不能由版本号大小或某个历史 README 的最低要求推断所有组合都可用。编译缓存还需绑定目标能力和相关编译条件；拷贝已编译文件不保证另一类设备能执行。\n\n镜像携带的用户态依赖与宿主提供的设备驱动、设备文件和资源控制属于不同层。问题可能表现为设备完全不可见、库初始化失败、某个 kernel 路径不支持，或通信路径降为另一种传输。应记录实际选择，不能只写“使用 GPU”或“启用 RDMA”。\n\n[NVIDIA device plugin v0.16.1](../sources/S-NVIDIA-PLUGIN.md) 显示设备清单可以经环境变量、挂载或 CDI 方式交给容器运行时，且设备身份有 UUID/index 选择。这说明部署分配、容器可见性与执行器里的逻辑序号需要形成映射。重启后逻辑 device 0 仍叫 0，不证明仍是之前那张物理卡或同一 MIG 资源。\n\n## CPU、内存与拓扑限制\n\nCPU 线程池大小需要结合实际可用 CPU 集与带宽 quota。设宿主有 64 个逻辑 CPU，但该服务的 CPU 预算为每 100 ms 最多消耗 200 ms 执行时间，长期上限相当于 2 个 CPU 的时间；把线程池设成 64 不会获得 64 核持续计算。[Linux cgroup v2](../sources/S-LINUX-RESOURCES.md) 还规定限制沿层级收紧，子组设置不能突破祖先限制。\n\n若设定分词每请求消耗 10 ms CPU、其他主机工作消耗 6 ms CPU，150 请求/s 需要 2.4 CPU 秒/s，已超出上述 2 CPU 预算。即使 GPU 很空闲，也不能先推断模型 kernel 是瓶颈。需要测 cpu.stat 的节流、实际队列和线程时间，并考虑增大主机份额或减少主机工作。\n\n内存预算包含匿名缓冲、文件缓存、共享内存和相关内核资源。设加载时源权重 24 GiB、转换后权重 24 GiB、staging 4 GiB 同时存活，则主项为 52 GiB；宿主拥有 512 GiB 也无法使一个上限 48 GiB 的服务自动加载成功。可采用已验证的流式转换降低峰值，或调整合法资源配额。\n\ncpuset 的 CPU 与内存节点设置、实际 NUMA 分配、NIC/SSD/GPU 亲和共同决定数据路径。指定核集合并不自动保证页面已在期望节点；改变内存节点可能触发昂贵且不完全的页面迁移。部署验证应读取有效值和实际路径，而不是只保留期望配置。\n\n## 健康、就绪与可接收容量\n\n健康探测回答进程是否能够推进其必要工作；就绪探测回答该模型版本和执行组是否已满足接收契约。端口能连接、HTTP 返回成功或插件进程没有退出，都不能单独证明权重完成加载、通信组完整、设备可用和代表路径正确。\n\n就绪还可以有资源范围：允许短文本但尚未准备长图像路径，或者 KV 已满而需要暂停准入。若把暂时过载当成进程失效并不断重启，就会清空热状态、重复加载并放大存储压力。无法恢复的设备或执行组错误则应退出相应可用集合，按[恢复](serving-recovery.md)协议重建。\n\n共享内存容量、文件描述符、进程/线程限制、I/O 限额和网络访问范围也需要进入环境检查。只提升 GPU 数量可能同时增加 tokenizer 进程、IPC 缓冲和并发制品读取，从而先耗尽这些资源。限制的数值要绑定实际配置，不在知识正文中写成通用默认值。\n\n## 更新与独立验证\n\n更换驱动、设备插件或运行时可能影响整个节点，而不只是一个服务进程。固定插件文档明确建议更新插件前排空 GPU 任务，并不保证任务在滚动升级中存活。具体升级策略应由目标版本与设备支持决定，先停止新准入，再完成或迁移已有工作，确认最后访问结束后释放旧环境。\n\n验收分三层：核对真实环境身份与有效资源；运行覆盖目标 shape、dtype、通信与状态路径的最小模型参考；重放代表负载验证服务质量和资源峰值。再测试 CPU 限额收紧、加载超出主存、设备映射变化、部分 rank 未就绪和实例排空。每次失败都应能区分环境不相容、配置不足与实现错误。\n\n当任务只在单一受控机器运行时，直接部署可以减少组织成本；多租户、多版本与弹性环境需要更明确的资源和生命周期契约。无论部署载体是什么，实验都要在与目标一致的有效资源范围内比较，避免把环境改变当成内核优化收益。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- "reading_scope": "excerpt", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:2146]\n# K-SYS-010：系统设计推导：把模型、缓存、执行与目标组合起来\n\n专家指导的产物不是一组彼此独立的优化提示，而是一条从任务条件到系统结构的可反驳推导链：状态模型、资源模型、候选设计、成本下界、证据状态和能区分竞争解释的实验。候选必须满足函数、身份、资源和完成条件，才有资格进入性能比较。下界通过只表示尚未被排除；上界未通过只表示尚未证明达标。两者都不能直接替代实际结果。\n\n## 统一问题表述：从条件到设计证书\n\n把设计条件写成 C＝(F,W,H,N,Q,S,E)。F 是模型函数、参数、位置规则、数值语义和必需状态；W 是到达过程、输入输出长度、前缀复用和会话依赖；H 是处理器、内存层级、互连、存储与拓扑；N 是运行时、编译器、驱动和可用执行路径；Q 是质量与数值容差；S 是首内容、逐 token、完成时间、吞吐、拒绝与公平目标；E 是已有来源事实、推导和测量及其作用域。设计变量 d 可以包含状态布局、缓存身份、调度粒度、执行后端、并行策略、阶段划分、队列和准入。\n\n输出不是单一总分，而是对每个条件格 k 和候选 d 给出设计证书：合法性、状态转移、各资源成本、证据支持的上下界、质量状态、排除理由，以及下一步区分实验。条件格是 F、W、H、N 的有限代表区域，例如模型版本、批大小、历史长度与共享或独立内存域的组合。格内连续变化的量仍须保留范围；中心点的结果不能自动覆盖整个格。\n\n设请求 r 的已知 token 数为 nᵣ，已提交且各必要层完整、可复用的 KV 终点为 cᵣ，满足 0≤cᵣ≤nᵣ。已生成文本不等于对应 KV 已计算；物理页已提交也不等于所有异步读者已经结束。系统在时刻 t 的驻留量为\n\nM(t)＝W_phys(t)＋Σ_unique P_state(t)＋A_work(t)＋I_flight(t)＋Q_queued(t)，\n\n其中 W_phys 是各物理副本的权重，P_state 是按存储域去重的状态页，A_work 是工作区，I_flight 是在途传输或暂定写，Q_queued 是消费者尚未释放的输出缓冲。同一物理 backing 在这些项间不重复计数。容量条件是 max_t M(t)≤M_cap；各项各自峰值之和只是保守上界，未必能在同一时刻达到。\n\n设计还必须固定比较的时间量 θ。它可以是某次固定执行的完成时间、预先指定有限回放的最大 ITL，或某个到达分布下的总体平均值、p99。它们是不同对象。下界、上界、观测和目标只有指向相同 θ，或存在明确的蕴含关系时，才能共同裁决候选。\n\n## 设计对象、合法性与证据状态\n\n合法性至少包含四层。函数合法性要求候选实现任务规定的模型函数、mask、位置、归一化和输出；数值合法性要求 dtype、累加、缩放、舍入和容差契约相容；设备语义要求布局、指令、可见性和完成事件支持所选路径；身份与并发合法性要求请求、版本、页、adapter、输出和迟到通知不会串用。任何一层失败都不能用平均更快抵消。允许近似的任务可以比较不同表示或求解轨迹，但必须把相应质量门槛写入 Q。\n\n普通稠密 GQA decoder 每 token 全层 KV 字节为\n\nb_KV＝2·L·H_kv·d_h·s，\n\nL 是层数，H_kv 是 KV head 数，d_h 是每 head 维数，s 是每元素字节。MLA 的持久 latent 不同，可按 L·(d_c＋d_Rh)·s 计算每 token 缓存主项。专家权重、临时分组激活、通信区与输出头另列。公式只描述声明的状态表示，不证明后端已选择相应 kernel。\n\n逻辑共享不等于物理共享，物理共享也不等于每次读取只发生一次。多个请求映射到同一物理前缀页时，容量账计一次；没有跨请求广播、片上驻留或其他复用契约时，各请求 attention 仍须取得自身可见历史，流量账不能直接去重。将共享页从流量中无证据扣除会低估带宽时间；把每次逻辑读取都当成新物理页又会高估容量。\n\n证书分别保存时间结论、质量状态与过期证据。下表的标签是主决策，原始证据及其他轴不因选择一个主标签而丢失。\n\n| 标签 | 含义与处理 | 不能推出 |\n|---|---|---|\n| `ILLEGAL_LEGALITY` | 已确认硬合法条件失败；排除当前候选与条件格的组合 | 修改表示或算法后的新候选也非法 |\n| `ILLEGAL_CAPACITY` | 已确认该执行计划必需的峰值驻留超过容量 | 仅因一个宽松容量上界超预算就证明不可容纳 |\n| `EXCLUDED_LOWER_BOUND` | 有效时间下界超过目标，且未发现同作用域矛盾证据 | 其他条件格也应排除 |\n| `EXCLUDED_MEASURED` | 相应作用域的直接观测或获准统计证据支持时间失败 | 任意保守上界超过目标即可排除 |\n| `EXCLUDED_QUALITY` | 同作用域质量证据判定失败；当前组合不能满足联合目标 | 提升速度能抵消质量失败 |\n| `LOWER_BOUND_SURVIVOR` | 当前未证明失败，也未证明时间与质量全部通过；保留开放候选 | 已满足 SLO |\n| `QU\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 12000:19298]\n 需要一起检验的关系 |\n|---|---|---|\n| C01 CPU 多请求生成 | 本章 CPU 场景 | 权重/KV 流量、线程与 prefill 时间片、开放到达和 ITL |\n| C02 单 GPU 交互 | [CUDA 平台](../hardware/cuda-execution.md) | 容量、带宽、内核与主机间隙、准入和输出 |\n| C03 长上下文高复用 | [前缀缓存](../algorithms/prefix-cache-identity.md) | 物理共享容量、逻辑读取、路由与长尾 |\n| C04 显存不足与三级存储 | [多级驻留](../algorithms/tiered-residency.md) | 有效副本、预取、共享带宽、双缓冲与恢复 |\n| C05 CPU–GPU 混合执行 | [异构计算](../algorithms/heterogeneous-execution.md) | 计算放置、传输路径、合并与主机竞争 |\n| C06 多卡 MLA/MoE | [多设备并行](../algorithms/parallelism.md) | 逐 rank 容量、head/latent/专家归属、通信与偏斜 |\n| C07 P/D 与远端 KV | [分离服务](../algorithms/disaggregated-serving.md) | 首内容、第二 token、迁移提交与池配比 |\n| C08 多模型/LoRA | [多模型放置](../workloads/multi-model-placement.md) | 共同基座、adapter/KV、冷加载与租户等待 |\n| C09 流式语音 | [转写](../models/encoder-decoder-streaming.md)、[语音生成](../models/speech-generation.md) | 稳定文字、可播放音频、阶段状态与消费时钟 |\n| C10 VLM/全模态 | [视觉语言](../models/vision-language.md)、[全模态](../models/omni-multimodal.md) | 媒体处理、特征与前缀缓存、分支同步和打断 |\n| C11 图像视频扩散 | [图像扩散](../models/image-diffusion.md)、[视频生成](../models/video-generation.md) | 求解器、CFG、时空切分、VAE 与媒体质量 |\n| C12 非生成推理 | [embedding/重排](../models/embedding-reranking.md)、[检索/RAG](retrieval-pipeline.md)、[视觉识别](../models/vision-inference.md)、[推荐](../models/recommendation-inference.md)、[GNN](../models/graph-inference.md) | 各自输入、状态与任务指标；五种契约分别验证 |\n\n场景可以交叉：RAG 调用 embedding、检索、重排与生成，多模态服务同时需要多模型放置和低延迟音频。组合时重新计算共同资源与状态，不能把各场景的独占带宽或独立质量结果直接相加。\n\n## 实例方案要能落到服务和平台\n\n多节点任务的执行组可能受 [集群放置](../workloads/cluster-resource-placement.md)拓扑与故障余量影响；共享制品、缓存和提交记录引入 [存储服务](storage-services.md)的相应语义。它们由候选实现还是环境提供，取决于任务；单机和离线引擎不因此自动增加功能要求。[部署环境](deployment-environment.md)说明真实配额、设备和版本，进程存活、文件存在或空闲卡数各自只提供局部信息。\n\n这些示例通过任务条件、状态、计算、流量、容量、交互与观测解释方案。其他设计可以采用不同表示和组织；没有恢复或请求排队的任务不需要补造对应模块。已有优化可以按需求采用、重组或省略，结论取决于实际前提和证据。\n\n## 设计例子的适用范围与未列路径\n\n上述场景不穷尽架构或设计变量。Task 决定交付的函数、质量、性能与资源边界；一个例子的调度器、状态池、阶段和验证顺序不会自动成为另一任务的要求。独立调度、整体编译、持久执行和其他组织方式应按实际结果判断。\n\n机制依赖与研发顺序不同。消费者需要有效输入，不等于工程上必须先冻结全部接口；可以用独立实验检查成本假设，再联合修正模型、表示、融合边界和调度。验证保留实际覆盖范围，最终按任务验收。\n\n候选可以扩展、组合或全部放弃。新方案没有现成章节或来源实现并不失格；改变表示、硬件能力或任务质量后，原先的排除前提也可能不再成立。阅读来源、符合例子步骤或获得状态标签本身不构成正确或更优的证据。\n\n## 布局改变后的完整成本与反转条件\n\n以下是设定反例。原路径在输入已满足布局时计算 1.0 ms，新 kernel 计算 0.6 ms，但需要 0.5 ms 输入转换；两者串行且不能隐藏时，一次执行为 1.1 ms。同一合法转换结果可复用 R 次时，新路径总成本为 0.5＋0.6R ms，原路径为 R ms，严格获益要求 R>1.25；R＝2 时为 1.7 对 2.0 ms。\n\n上游直接产生目标布局、融合转换与计算或改变状态表示，都会改变成本项。输入每次改变则复用前提失效；转换与其他消费者争用带宽也要重算。该公式判断声明的候选，不排除能改变其前提的新设计。准备时间的保守上界超过收益预算时，同样不能冒充实际一定更慢的证据。\n\n## 区分实验、反证与修正\n\n| 观察 | 竞争解释 | 区分干预与预测 | 反证后的修正 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 命中率上升但队列更长 | 亲和集中；短 query 主机成本；保留页挤占容量 | 控制实际命中边界后改路由；固定命中改 query；固定计算增加容量 | 按真正改变结果的干预修路由、准备或保留策略 |\n| batch 增大后 token/s 上升而 ITL 超标 | 权重摊薄；轮转等待；prefill 干扰 | 固定历史改变 batch，分记每轮与请求等待；单独开关分块 | 收窄 batch、准入或阶段组织，不把总吞吐当 SLO |\n| 两副本没有加速 | 共享带宽；权重复制；路由集中；锁或输出竞争 | 同流量比较共享域与独立域，记录带宽、份额与等待 | 独立域改善才支持带宽解释，否则检查其余路径 |\n| 位宽下降而时间不降 | 解包、转换、归约、fallback；主机准备；事务未降 | 固定形状与质量门槛，分别改变位宽、kernel 与线程，核对实际路径 | 撤回不受观察支持的带宽归因，数值与质量另验 |\n| 图重放仍有主机间隙 | 捕获不完整；缓冲更新；输入等待 | 固定请求改变捕获边界，分记 plan/replay，预填输入 | 准备增长则修准备，仍排队则查准入与供给 |\n| 上界 80 ms 超目标 60 ms | 上界宽松；实际确实超标 | 取得同作用域观测或收紧上界；50 ms 的合法结果能反驳“上界超标即失败” | 保留开放候选，只有有效失败观测或下界才支持排除 |\n| 耗时证据通过但不能认定合格 | 质量失败；质量未知或过期 | 固定候选，用既定独立参考与任务评估裁决质量 | 失败排除当前组合，未知保留待验，均禁止 VERIFIED |\n| 观测或上界低于声明下界 | 单位、计时窗、读取层级或缓存前提错误；统计方法失配 | 核对对象与起止，隔离阶段并使用独立计数或参考 | 先转 INCONSISTENT，修前提后重算，不先用矛盾下界删候选 |\n\n实验单位应是独立运行、条件格或预定配对区组，同一运行的相关 token 不自动独立。报告原始到达、完成、拒绝、超时和队列增长；不能只挑成功请求均值。一次干预同时改变 routing、缓存和 batch 时，需控制或报告这些变化。\n\n随章 `tools/check_k_sys_010_full.py` 是标准库、确定性、离线、只读的软件模型。它用独立物理 token 槽集合与逐请求读取枚举核对容量和流量，用有理数核对 CPU 设定算例，再以有限可能耗时集合检验分类。负对照实际执行错误分类分支，包括宽上界当失败、忽略质量、矛盾检查之前按下界排除和下界当通过；集合参考必须拒绝这些结果。检查还覆盖作用域失配、阈值相等、区间跨阈值和直接观测。\n\n该检查不产生统计区间，不证明输入证据真实或区间覆盖有效，不执行真实 forward、异步物理回收、CPU/GPU 性能或质量实验。分类枚举使用有限整数耗时域；另有明确的 39.0625 ms 有理数反例。目标平台的同步、事务、总体尾延迟和质量继续需要相应证据。\n\n## 从推导到 RSI 增量\n\n规划指出链条中最不确定的关系，执行实现对应机制，诊断核对预测与实际状态。进化把新认识写回受影响机制：补足成本、修正恢复条件或组合分支。成功不必产生新条目，更精确理解现有关系本身就是增量。\n\n写回记录对象身份、依赖边、单位、条件格和证据口径。例如共享页降低容量与降低每请求读取是不同命题；保守上界超过目标与实际失败也是不同命题。页表唯一化支持前者的容量关系，广播或片上复用才支持读取去重；同作用域失败观测或下界才支持排除。把局部实验时间差或一个宽松界直接写成通用机制，会把证据范围扩成它未证明的结论。\n\n<!-- extracted-character range 0:68728]\n=== PDF page 1 ===\nLarge-scale cluster management at Google with Borg\nAbhishek Verma†\nLuis Pedrosa‡\nMadhukar Korupolu\nDavid Oppenheimer\nEric Tune\nJohn Wilkes\nGoogle Inc.\nAbstract\nGoogle’s Borg system is a cluster manager that runs hun-\ndreds of thousands of jobs, from many thousands of differ-\nent applications, across a number of clusters each with up to\ntens of thousands of machines.\nIt achieves high utilization by combining admission con-\ntrol, efﬁcient task-packing, over-commitment, and machine\nsharing with process-level performance isolation. It supports\nhigh-availability applications with runtime features that min-\nimize fault-recovery time, and scheduling policies that re-\nduce the probability of correlated failures. Borg simpliﬁes\nlife for its users by offering a declarative job speciﬁcation\nlanguage, name service integration, real-time job monitor-\ning, and tools to analyze and simulate system behavior.\nWe present a summary of the Borg system architecture\nand features, important design decisions, a quantitative anal-\nysis of some of its policy decisions, and a qualitative ex-\namination of lessons learned from a decade of operational\nexperience with it.\n1.\nIntroduction\nThe cluster management system we internally call Borg ad-\nmits, schedules, starts, restarts, and monitors the full range\nof applications that Google runs. This paper explains how.\nBorg provides three main beneﬁts: it (1) hides the details\nof resource management and failure handling so its users can\nfocus on application development instead; (2) operates with\nvery high reliability and availability, and supports applica-\ntions that do the same; and (3) lets us run workloads across\ntens of thousands of machines effectively. Borg is not the\nﬁrst system to address these issues, but it’s one of the few op-\nerating at this scale, with this degree of resiliency and com-\npleteness. This paper is organized around these topics, con-\n† Work done while author was at Google.\n‡ Currently at University of Southern California.\nPermission to make digital or hard copies of part or all of this work for personal or\nclassroom use is granted without fee provided that copies are not made or distributed\nfor proﬁt or commercial advantage and that copies bear this notice and the full citation\non the ﬁrst page. Copyrights for third-party components of this work must be honored.\nFor all other uses, contact the owner/author(s).\nEuroSys’15,\nApril 21–24, 2015, Bordeaux, France.\nCopyright is held by the owner/author(s).\nACM 978-1-4503-3238-5/15/04.\nhttp://dx.doi.org/10.1145/2741948.2741964\nweb browsers\nBorgMaster\nlink shard\nUI shard\nBorgMaster\nlink shard\nUI shard\nBorgMaster\nlink shard\nUI shard\nBorgMaster\nlink shard\nUI shard\nCell\nScheduler\nborgcfg\ncommand-line \ntools\nweb browsers\nscheduler\nBorglet\nBorglet\nBorglet\nBorglet\nBorgMaster\nlink shard\nread/UI \nshard\nconfig \nfile\npersistent store \n(Paxos)\nFigure 1: The high-level architecture of Borg. Only a tiny fraction\nof the thousands of worker nodes are shown.\ncluding with a set of qualitative observations we have made\nfrom operating Borg in production for more than a decade.\n2.\nThe user perspective\nBorg’s users are Google developers and system administra-\ntors (site reliability engineers or SREs) that run Google’s\napplications and services. Users submit their work to Borg\nin the form of jobs, each of which consists of one or more\ntasks that all run the same program (binary). Each job runs\nin one Borg cell, a set of machines that are managed as a\nunit. The remainder of this section describes the main fea-\ntures exposed in the user view of Borg.\n2.1\nThe workload\nBorg cells run a heterogenous workload with two main parts.\nThe ﬁrst is long-running services that should “never” go\ndown, and handle short-lived latency-sensitive requests (a\nfew µs to a few hundred ms). Such services are used for\nend-user-facing products such as Gmail, Google Docs, and\nweb search, and for internal infrastructure services (e.g.,\nBigTable). The second is batch jobs that take from a few\nseconds to a few days to complete; these are much less sen-\nsitive to short-term performance ﬂuctuations. The workload\nmix varies across cells, which run different mixes of applica-\ntions depending on their major tenants (e.g., some cells are\nquite batch-intensive), and also varies over time: batch jobs\n\n\n=== PDF page 2 ===\ncome and go, and many end-user-facing service jobs see a\ndiurnal usage pattern. Borg is required to handle all these\ncases equally well.\nA representative Borg workload can be found in a publicly-\navailable month-long trace from May 2011 [80], which has\nbeen extensively analyzed (e.g., [68] and [1, 26, 27, 57]).\nMany application frameworks have been built on top of\nBorg over the last few years, including our internal MapRe-\nduce system [23], FlumeJava [18], Millwheel [3], and Pregel\n[59]. Most of these have a controller that submits a master\njob and one or more worker jobs; the ﬁrst two play a similar\nrole to YARN’s application manager [76]. Our distributed\nstorage systems such as GFS [34] and its successor CFS,\nBigtable [19], and Megastore [8] all run on Borg.\nFor this paper, we classify higher-priority Borg jobs as\n“production” (prod) ones, and the rest as “non-production”\n(non-prod). Most long-running server jobs are prod; most\nbatch jobs are non-prod. In a representative cell, prod jobs\nare allocated about 70% of the total CPU resources and rep-\nresent about 60% of the total CPU usage; they are allocated\nabout 55% of the total memory and represent about 85% of\nthe total memory usage. The discrepancies between alloca-\ntion and usage will prove important in §5.5.\n2.2\nClusters and cells\nThe machines in a cell belong to a single cluster, deﬁned by\nthe high-performance datacenter-scale network fabric that\nconnects them. A cluster lives inside a single datacenter\nbuilding, and a collection of buildings makes up a site.1\nA cluster usually hosts one large cell and may have a few\nsmaller-scale test or special-purpose cells. We assiduously\navoid any single point of failure.\nOur median cell size is about 10 k machines after exclud-\ning test cells; some are much larger. The machines in a cell\nare heterogeneous in many dimensions: sizes (CPU, RAM,\ndisk, network), processor type, performance, and capabili-\nties such as an external IP address or ﬂash storage. Borg iso-\nlates users from most of these differences by determining\nwhere in a cell to run tasks, allocating their resources, in-\nstalling their programs and other dependencies, monitoring\ntheir health, and restarting them if they fail.\n2.3\nJobs and tasks\nA Borg job’s properties include its name, owner, and the\nnumber of tasks it has. Jobs can have constraints to force\nits tasks to run on machines with particular attributes such as\nprocessor architecture, OS version, or an external IP address.\nConstraints can be hard or soft; the latter act like preferences\nrather than requirements. The start of a job can be deferred\nuntil a prior one ﬁnishes. A job runs in just one cell.\nEach task maps to a set of Linux processes running in\na container on a machine [62]. The vast majority of the\nBorg workload does not run inside virtual machines (VMs),\n1 There are a few exceptions for each of these relationships.\nbecause we don’t want to pay the cost of virtualization.\nAlso, the system was designed at a time when we had a\nconsiderable investment in processors with no virtualization\nsupport in hardware.\nA task has properties too, such as its resource require-\nments and the task’s index within the job. Most task proper-\nties are the same across all tasks in a job, but can be over-\nridden – e.g., to provide task-speciﬁc command-line ﬂags.\nEach resource dimension (CPU cores, RAM, disk space,\ndisk access rate, TCP ports,2 etc.) is speciﬁed independently\nat ﬁne granularity; we don’t impose ﬁxed-sized buckets or\nslots (§5.4). Borg programs are statically linked to reduce\ndependencies on their runtime environment, and structured\nas packages of binaries and data ﬁles, whose installation is\norchestrated by Borg.\nUsers operate on jobs by issuing remote procedure calls\n(RPCs) to Borg, most commonly from a command-line tool,\nother Borg jobs, or our monitoring systems (§2.6). Most job\ndescriptions are written in the declarative conﬁguration lan-\nguage BCL. This is a variant of GCL [12], which gener-\nates protobuf ﬁles [67], extended with some Borg-speciﬁc\nkeywords. GCL provides lambda functions to allow calcula-\ntions, and these are used by applications to adjust their con-\nﬁgurations to their environment; tens of thousands of BCL\nﬁles are over 1 k lines long, and we have accumulated tens\nof millions of lines of BCL. Borg job conﬁgurations have\nsimilarities to Aurora conﬁguration ﬁles [6].\nFigure 2 illustrates the states that jobs and tasks go\nthrough during their lifetime.\nsubmit + \naccept\nPending\nRunning\nDead\nupdate\nschedule\nupdate\nfinish, fail, kill, lost\nsubmit\nfail, kill, \nlost\nevict\nreject\nFigure 2: The state diagram for both jobs and tasks. Users can\ntrigger submit, kill, and update transitions.\nA user can change the properties of some or all of the\ntasks in a running job by pushing a new job conﬁguration\nto Borg, and then instructing Borg to update the tasks to\nthe new speciﬁcation. This acts as a lightweight, non-atomic\ntransaction that can easily be undone until it is closed (com-\nmitted). Updates are generally done in a rolling fashion, and\na limit can be imposed on the number of task disruptions\n2 Borg manages the available ports on a machine and allocates them to tasks.\n\n\n=== PDF page 3 ===\n(reschedules or preemptions) an update causes; any changes\nthat would cause more disruptions are skipped.\nSome task updates (e.g., pushing a new binary) will al-\nways require the task to be restarted; some (e.g., increasing\nresource requirements or changing constraints) might make\nthe task no longer ﬁt on the machine, and cause it to be\nstopped and rescheduled; and some (e.g., changing priority)\ncan always be done without restarting or moving the task.\nTasks can ask to be notiﬁed via a Unix SIGTERM sig-\nnal before they are preempted by a SIGKILL, so they have\ntime to clean up, save state, ﬁnish any currently-executing\nrequests, and decline new ones. The actual notice may be\nless if the preemptor sets a delay bound. In practice, a notice\nis delivered about 80% of the time.\n2.4\nAllocs\nA Borg alloc (short for allocation) is a reserved set of re-\nsources on a machine in which one or more tasks can be\nrun; the resources remain assigned whether or not they are\nused. Allocs can be used to set resources aside for future\ntasks, to retain resources between stopping a task and start-\ning it again, and to gather tasks from different jobs onto the\nsame machine – e.g., a web server instance and an associ-\nated logsaver task that copies the server’s URL logs from\nthe local disk to a distributed ﬁle system. The resources of\nan alloc are treated in a similar way to the resources of a ma-\nchine; multiple tasks running inside one share its resources.\nIf an alloc must be relocated to another machine, its tasks are\nrescheduled with it.\nAn alloc set is like a job: it is a group of allocs that reserve\nresources on multiple machines. Once an alloc set has been\ncreated, one or more jobs can be submitted to run in it. For\nbrevity, we will generally use “task” to refer to an alloc or a\ntop-level task (one outside an alloc) and “job” to refer to a\njob or alloc set.\n2.5\nPriority, quota, and admission control\nWhat happens when more work shows up than can be ac-\ncommodated? Our solutions for this are priority and quota.\nEvery job has a priority, a small positive integer. A high-\npriority task can obtain resources at the expense of a lower-\npriority one, even if that involves preempting (killing) the\nlatter. Borg deﬁnes non-overlapping priority bands for dif-\nferent uses, including (in decreasing-priority order): moni-\ntoring, production, batch, and best eﬀort (also known as\ntesting or free). For this paper, prod jobs are the ones in the\nmonitoring and production bands.\nAlthough a preempted task will often be rescheduled\nelsewhere in the cell, preemption cascades could occur if\na high-priority task bumped out a slightly lower-priority\none, which bumped out another slightly-lower priority task,\nand so on. To eliminate most of this, we disallow tasks in\nthe production priority band to preempt one another. Fine-\ngrained priorities are still useful in other circumstances –\ne.g., MapReduce master tasks run at a slightly higher priority\nthan the workers they control, to improve their reliability.\nPriority expresses relative importance for jobs that are\nrunning or waiting to run in a cell. Quota is used to decide\nwhich jobs to admit for scheduling. Quota is expressed as\na vector of resource quantities (CPU, RAM, disk, etc.) at a\ngiven priority, for a period of time (typically months). The\nquantities specify the maximum amount of resources that\na user’s job requests can ask for at a time (e.g., “20 TiB\nof RAM at prod priority from now until the end of July\nin cell xx”). Quota-checking is part of admission control,\nnot scheduling: jobs with insufﬁcient quota are immediately\nrejected upon submission.\nHigher-priority quota costs more than quota at lower-\npriority. Production-priority quota is limited to the actual\nresources available in the cell, so that a user who submits\na production-priority job that ﬁts in their quota can expect it\nto run, modulo fragmentation and constraints. Even though\nwe encourage users to purchase no more quota than they\nneed, many users overbuy because it insulates them against\nfuture shortages when their application’s user base grows.\nWe respond to this by over-selling quota at lower-priority\nlevels: every user has inﬁnite quota at priority zero, although\nthis is frequently hard to exercise because resources are over-\nsubscribed. A low-priority job may be admitted but remain\npending (unscheduled) due to insufﬁcient resources.\nQuota allocation is handled outside of Borg, and is inti-\nmately tied to our physical capacity planning, whose results\nare reﬂected in the price and availability of quota in differ-\nent datacenters. User jobs are admitted only if they have suf-\nﬁcient quota at the required priority. The use of quota re-\nduces the need for policies like Dominant Resource Fairness\n(DRF) [29, 35, 36, 66].\nBorg has a capability system that gives special privileges\nto some users; for example, allowing administrators to delete\nor modify any job in the cell, or allowing a user to access\nrestricted kernel features or Borg behaviors such as disabling\nresource estimation (§5.5) on their jobs.\n2.6\nNaming and monitoring\nIt’s not enough to create and place tasks: a service’s clients\nand other systems need to be able to ﬁnd them, even after\nthey are relocated to a new machine. To enable this, Borg\ncreates a stable “Borg name service” (BNS) name for each\ntask that includes the cell name, job name, and task number.\nBorg writes the task’s hostname and port into a consistent,\nhighly-available ﬁle in Chubby [14] with this name, which\nis used by our RPC system to ﬁnd the task endpoint. The\nBNS name also forms the basis of the task’s DNS name,\nso the ﬁftieth task in job jfoo owned by user ubar in cell\ncc would be reachable via 50.jfoo.ubar.cc.borg.google.com.\nBorg also writes job size and task health information into\nChubby whenever it changes, so load balancers can see\nwhere to route requests to.\n\n\n=== PDF page 4 ===\nAlmost every task run under Borg contains a built-in\nHTTP server that publishes information about the health of\nthe task and thousands of performance metrics (e.g., RPC\nlatencies). Borg monitors the health-check URL and restarts\ntasks that do not respond promptly or return an HTTP er-\nror code. Other data is tracked by monitoring tools for dash-\nboards and alerts on service level objective (SLO) violations.\nA service called Sigma provides a web-based user inter-\nface (UI) through which a user can examine the state of all\ntheir jobs, a particular cell, or drill down to individual jobs\nand tasks to examine their resource behavior, detailed logs,\nexecution history, and eventual fate. Our applications gener-\nate voluminous logs; these are automatically rotated to avoid\nrunning out of disk space, and preserved for a while after the\ntask’s exit to assist with debugging. If a job is not running\nBorg provides a “why pending?” annotation, together with\nguidance on how to modify the job’s resource requests to\nbetter ﬁt the cell. We publish guidelines for “conforming”\nresource shapes that are likely to schedule easily.\nBorg records all job submissions and task events, as well\nas detailed per-task resource usage information in Infrastore,\na scalable read-only data store with an interactive SQL-like\ninterface via Dremel [61]. This data is used for usage-based\ncharging, debugging job and system failures, and long-term\ncapacity planning. It also provided the data for the Google\ncluster workload trace [80].\nAll of these features help users to understand and debug\nthe behavior of Borg and their jobs, and help our SREs\nmanage a few tens of thousands of machines per person.\n3.\nBorg architecture\nA Borg cell consists of a set of machines, a logically central-\nized controller called the Borgmaster, and an agent process\ncalled the Borglet that runs on each machine in a cell (see\nFigure 1). All components of Borg are written in C++.\n3.1\nBorgmaster\nEach cell’s Borgmaster consists of two processes: the main\nBorgmaster process and a separate scheduler (§3.2). The\nmain Borgmaster process handles client RPCs that either\nmutate state (e.g., create job) or provide read-only access\nto data (e.g., lookup job). It also manages state machines\nfor all of the objects in the system (machines, tasks, allocs,\netc.), communicates with the Borglets, and offers a web UI\nas a backup to Sigma.\nThe Borgmaster is logically a single process but is ac-\ntually replicated ﬁve times. Each replica maintains an in-\nmemory copy of most of the state of the cell, and this state is\nalso recorded in a highly-available, distributed, Paxos-based\nstore [55] on the replicas’ local disks. A single elected mas-\nter per cell serves both as the Paxos leader and the state\nmutator, handling all operations that change the cell’s state,\nsuch as submitting a job or terminating a task on a ma-\nchine. A master is elected (using Paxos) when the cell is\nbrought up and whenever the elected master fails; it acquires\na Chubby lock so other systems can ﬁnd it. Electing a master\nand failing-over to the new one typically takes about 10 s, but\ncan take up to a minute in a big cell because some in-memory\nstate has to be reconstructed. When a replica recovers from\nan outage, it dynamically re-synchronizes its state from other\nPaxos replicas that are up-to-date.\nThe Borgmaster’s state at a point in time is called a\ncheckpoint, and takes the form of a periodic snapshot plus a\nchange log kept in the Paxos store. Checkpoints have many\nuses, including restoring a Borgmaster’s state to an arbitrary\npoint in the past (e.g., just before accepting a request that\ntriggered a software defect in Borg so it can be debugged);\nﬁxing it by hand in extremis; building a persistent log of\nevents for future queries; and ofﬂine simulations.\nA high-ﬁdelity Borgmaster simulator called Fauxmaster\ncan be used to read checkpoint ﬁles, and contains a complete\ncopy of the production Borgmaster code, with stubbed-out\ninterfaces to the Borglets. It accepts RPCs to make state ma-\nchine changes and perform operations, such as “schedule all\npending tasks”, and we use it to debug failures, by interact-\ning with it as if it were a live Borgmaster, with simulated\nBorglets replaying real interactions from the checkpoint ﬁle.\nA user can step through and observe the changes to the sys-\ntem state that actually occurred in the past. Fauxmaster is\nalso useful for capacity planning (“how many new jobs of\nthis type would ﬁt?”), as well as sanity checks before mak-\ning a change to a cell’s conﬁguration (“will this change evict\nany important jobs?”).\n3.2\nScheduling\nWhen a job is submitted, the Borgmaster records it persis-\ntently in the Paxos store and adds the job’s tasks to the pend-\ning queue. This is scanned asynchronously by the scheduler,\nwhich assigns tasks to machines if there are sufﬁcient avail-\nable resources that meet the job’s constraints. (The sched-\nuler primarily operates on tasks, not jobs.) The scan pro-\nceeds from high to low priority, modulated by a round-robin\nscheme within a priority to ensure fairness across users and\navoid head-of-line blocking behind a large job. The schedul-\ning algorithm has two parts: feasibility checking, to ﬁnd ma-\nchines on which the task could run, and scoring, which picks\none of the feasible machines.\nIn feasibility checking, the scheduler ﬁnds a set of ma-\nchines that meet the task’s constraints and also have enough\n“available” resources – which includes resources assigned\nto lower-priority tasks that can be evicted. In scoring, the\nscheduler determines the “goodness” of each feasible ma-\nchine. The score takes into account user-speciﬁed prefer-\nences, but is mostly driven by built-in criteria such as mini-\nmizing the number and priority of preempted tasks, picking\nmachines that already have a copy of the task’s packages,\nspreading tasks across power and failure domains, and pack-\ning quality including putting a mix of high and low priority\n\n\n=== PDF page 5 ===\ntasks onto a single machine to allow the high-priority ones\nto expand in a load spike.\nBorg originally used a variant of E-PVM [4] for scoring,\nwhich generates a single cost value across heterogeneous\nresources and minimizes the change in cost when placing\na task. In practice, E-PVM ends up spreading load across\nall the machines, leaving headroom for load spikes – but at\nthe expense of increased fragmentation, especially for large\ntasks that need most of the machine; we sometimes call this\n“worst ﬁt”.\nThe opposite end of the spectrum is “best ﬁt”, which tries\nto ﬁll machines as tightly as possible. This leaves some ma-\nchines empty of user jobs (they still run storage servers), so\nplacing large tasks is straightforward, but the tight packing\npenalizes any mis-estimations in resource requirements by\nusers or Borg. This hurts applications with bursty loads, and\nis particularly bad for batch jobs which specify low CPU\nneeds so they can schedule easily and try to run opportunis-\ntically in unused resources: 20% of non-prod tasks request\nless than 0.1 CPU cores.\nOur current scoring model is a hybrid one that tries to\nreduce the amount of stranded resources – ones that cannot\nbe used because another resource on the machine is fully\nallocated. It provides about 3–5% better packing efﬁciency\n(deﬁned in [78]) than best ﬁt for our workloads.\nIf the machine selected by the scoring phase doesn’t have\nenough available resources to ﬁt the new task, Borg preempts\n(kills) lower-priority tasks, from lowest to highest priority,\nuntil it does. We add the preempted tasks to the scheduler’s\npending queue, rather than migrate or hibernate them.3\nTask startup latency (the time from job submission to\na task running) is an area that has received and continues\nto receive signiﬁcant attention. It is highly variable, with\nthe median typically about 25 s. Package installation takes\nabout 80% of the total: one of the known bottlenecks is\ncontention for the local disk where packages are written to.\nTo reduce task startup time, the scheduler prefers to assign\ntasks to machines that already have the necessary packages\n(programs and data) installed: most packages are immutable\nand so can be shared and cached. (This is the only form of\ndata locality supported by the Borg scheduler.) In addition,\nBorg distributes packages to machines in parallel using tree-\nand torrent-like protocols.\nAdditionally, the scheduler uses several techniques to let\nit scale up to cells with tens of thousands of machines (§3.4).\n3.3\nBorglet\nThe Borglet is a local Borg agent that is present on every\nmachine in a cell. It starts and stops tasks; restarts them if\nthey fail; manages local resources by manipulating OS ker-\nnel settings; rolls over debug logs; and reports the state of the\nmachine to the Borgmaster and other monitoring systems.\n3 Exception: tasks that provide virtual machines for Google Compute En-\ngine users are migrated.\nThe Borgmaster polls each Borglet every few seconds to\nretrieve the machine’s current state and send it any outstand-\ning requests. This gives Borgmaster control over the rate of\ncommunication, avoids the need for an explicit ﬂow control\nmechanism, and prevents recovery storms [9].\nThe elected master is responsible for preparing messages\nto send to the Borglets and for updating the cell’s state with\ntheir responses. For performance scalability, each Borgmas-\nter replica runs a stateless link shard to handle the communi-\ncation with some of the Borglets; the partitioning is recalcu-\nlated whenever a Borgmaster election occurs. For resiliency,\nthe Borglet always reports its full state, but the link shards\naggregate and compress this information by reporting only\ndifferences to the state machines, to reduce the update load\nat the elected master.\nIf a Borglet does not respond to several poll messages its\nmachine is marked as down and any tasks it was running\nare rescheduled on other machines. If communication is\nrestored the Borgmaster tells the Borglet to kill those tasks\nthat have been rescheduled, to avoid duplicates. A Borglet\ncontinues normal operation even if it loses contact with the\nBorgmaster, so currently-running tasks and services stay up\neven if all Borgmaster replicas fail.\n3.4\nScalability\nWe are not sure where the ultimate scalability limit to Borg’s\ncentralized architecture will come from; so far, every time\nwe have approached a limit, we’ve managed to eliminate it.\nA single Borgmaster can manage many thousands of ma-\nchines in a cell, and several cells have arrival rates above\n10 000 tasks per minute. A busy Borgmaster uses 10–14\nCPU cores and up to 50 GiB RAM. We use several tech-\nniques to achieve this scale.\nEarly versions of Borgmaster had a simple, synchronous\nloop that accepted requests, scheduled tasks, and commu-\nnicated with Borglets. To handle larger cells, we split the\nscheduler into a separate process so it could operate in paral-\nlel with the other Borgmaster functions that are replicated for\nfailure tolerance. A scheduler replica operates on a cached\ncopy of the cell state. It repeatedly: retrieves state changes\nfrom the elected master (including both assigned and pend-\ning work); updates its local copy; does a scheduling pass\nto assign tasks; and informs the elected master of those as-\nsignments. The master will accept and apply these assign-\nments unless they are inappropriate (e.g., based on out of\ndate state), which will cause them to be reconsidered in the\nscheduler’s next pass. This is quite similar in spirit to the\noptimistic concurrency control used in Omega [69], and in-\ndeed we recently added the ability for Borg to use different\nschedulers for different workload types.\nTo improve response times, we added separate threads\nto talk to the Borglets and respond to read-only RPCs. For\ngreater performance, we sharded (partitioned) these func-\ntions across the ﬁve Borgmaster replicas §3.3. Together,\n\n\n=== PDF page 6 ===\nprod \nnon-prod\n0\n1\n2\n3\n4\n5\n6\n7\n8\nEvictions per task-week\nmachine shutdown\nother\nout of resources\nmachine failure\npreemption\nFigure 3: Task-eviction rates and causes for production and non-\nproduction workloads. Data from August 1st 2013.\nthese keep the 99%ile response time of the UI below 1 s\nand the 95%ile of the Borglet polling interval below 10 s.\nSeveral things make the Borg scheduler more scalable:\nScore caching: Evaluating feasibility and scoring a ma-\nchine is expensive, so Borg caches the scores until the prop-\nerties of the machine or task change – e.g., a task on the ma-\nchine terminates, an attribute is altered, or a task’s require-\nments change. Ignoring small changes in resource quantities\nreduces cache invalidations.\nEquivalence classes: Tasks in a Borg job usually have\nidentical requirements and constraints, so rather than deter-\nmining feasibility for every pending task on every machine,\nand scoring all the feasible machines, Borg only does fea-\nsibility and scoring for one task per equivalence class – a\ngroup of tasks with identical requirements.\nRelaxed randomization: It is wasteful to calculate fea-\nsibility and scores for all the machines in a large cell, so the\nscheduler examines machines in a random order until it has\nfound “enough” feasible machines to score, and then selects\nthe best within that set. This reduces the amount of scoring\nand cache invalidations needed when tasks enter and leave\nthe system, and speeds up assignment of tasks to machines.\nRelaxed randomization is somewhat akin to the batch sam-\npling of Sparrow [65] while also handling priorities, preemp-\ntions, heterogeneity and the costs of package installation.\nIn our experiments (§5), scheduling a cell’s entire work-\nload from scratch typically took a few hundred seconds, but\ndid not ﬁnish after more than 3 days when the above tech-\nniques were disabled. Normally, though, an online schedul-\ning pass over the pending queue completes in less than half\na second.\n4.\nAvailability\nFailures are the norm in large scale systems [10, 11, 22].\nFigure 3 provides a breakdown of task eviction causes in\n15 sample cells. Applications that run on Borg are expected\nto handle such events, using techniques such as replication,\nstoring persistent state in a distributed ﬁle system, and (if\nappropriate) taking occasional checkpoints. Even so, we try\nto mitigate the impact of these events. For example, Borg:\n• automatically reschedules evicted tasks, on a new ma-\nchine if necessary;\n• reduces correlated failures by spreading tasks of a job\nacross failure domains such as machines, racks, and\npower domains;\n• limits the allowed rate of task disruptions and the number\nof tasks from a job that can be simultaneously down\n65\n70\n75\n80\n85\n90\n95\n100\n0\n20\n40\n60\n80\n100\nCompacted size [%]\nPercentage of cells\nFigure 4: The effects of compaction. A CDF of the percentage of\noriginal cell size achieved after compaction, across 15 cells.\nduring maintenance activities such as OS or machine\nupgrades;\n• uses declarative desired-state representations and idem-\npotent mutating operations, so that a failed client can\nharmlessly resubmit any forgotten requests;\n• rate-limits ﬁnding new places for tasks from machines\nthat become unreachable, because it cannot distinguish\nbetween large-scale machine failure and a network parti-\ntion;\n• avoids repeating task::machine pairings that cause task or\nmachine crashes; and\n• recovers critical intermediate data written to local disk by\nrepeatedly re-running a logsaver task (§2.4), even if the\nalloc it was attached to is terminated or moved to another\nmachine. Users can set how long the system keeps trying;\na few days is common.\nA key design feature in Borg is that already-running tasks\ncontinue to run even if the Borgmaster or a task’s Borglet\ngoes down. But keeping the master up is still important\nbecause when it is down new jobs cannot be submitted\nor existing ones updated, and tasks from failed machines\ncannot be rescheduled.\nBorgmaster uses a combination of techniques that enable\nit to achieve 99.99% availability in practice: replication for\nmachine failures; admission control to avoid overload; and\ndeploying instances using simple, low-level tools to mini-\nmize external dependencies. Each cell is independent of the\nothers to minimize the chance of correlated operator errors\nand failure propagation. These goals, not scalability limita-\ntions, are the primary argument against larger cells.\n5.\nUtilization\nOne of Borg’s primary goals is to make efﬁcient use of\nGoogle’s ﬂeet of machines, which represents a signiﬁcant\nﬁnancial investment: increasing utilization by a few percent-\nage points can save millions of dollars. This section dis-\ncusses and evaluates some of the policies and techniques that\nBorg uses to do so.\n\n\n=== PDF page 7 ===\nA\nB\nC\nD\nE\n0\n50\n100\n150\n200\nCell\nPercentage of cell\nprod\nnon-prod\nbaseline\nunused\n(a) The left column for each cell shows the original size and the\ncombined workload; the right one shows the segregated case.\n-10\n0\n10\n20\n30\n40\n50\n60\n0\n20\n40\n60\n80\n100\nOverhead from segregation [%]\nPercentage of cells\n(b) CDF of additional machines that would be needed if we\nsegregated the workload of 15 representative cells.\nFigure 5: Segregating prod and non-prod work into different cells would need more machines. Both graphs show how many extra machines\nwould be needed if the prod and non-prod workloads were sent to separate cells, expressed as a percentage of the minimum number of\nmachines required to run the workload in a single cell. In this, and subsequent CDF plots, the value shown for each cell is derived from the\n90%ile of the different cell sizes our experiment trials produced; the error bars show the complete range of values from the trials.\n5.1\nEvaluation methodology\nOur jobs have placement constraints and need to handle rare\nworkload spikes, our machines are heterogenous, and we\nrun batch jobs in resources reclaimed from service jobs. So,\nto evaluate our policy choices we needed a more sophisti-\ncated metric than “average utilization”. After much exper-\nimentation we picked cell compaction: given a workload,\nwe found out how small a cell it could be ﬁtted into by\nremoving machines until the workload no longer ﬁtted, re-\npeatedly re-packing the workload from scratch to ensure that\nwe didn’t get hung up on an unlucky conﬁguration. This\nprovided clean termination conditions and facilitated auto-\nmated comparisons without the pitfalls of synthetic work-\nload generation and modeling [31]. A quantitative compari-\nson of evaluation techniques can be found in [78]: the details\nare surprisingly subtle.\nIt wasn’t possible to perform experiments on live produc-\ntion cells, but we used Fauxmaster to obtain high-ﬁdelity\nsimulation results, using data from real production cells\nand workloads, including all their constraints, actual lim-\nits, reservations, and usage data (§5.5). This data came\nfrom Borg checkpoints taken on Wednesday 2014-10-01\n14:00 PDT. (Other checkpoints produced similar results.)\nWe picked 15 Borg cells to report on by ﬁrst eliminating\nspecial-purpose, test, and small (< 5000 machines) cells,\nand then sampled the remaining population to achieve a\nroughly even spread across the range of sizes.\nTo maintain machine heterogeneity in the compacted cell\nwe randomly selected machines to remove. To maintain\nworkload heterogeneity, we kept it all, except for server and\nstorage tasks tied to a particular machine (e.g., the Borglets).\nWe changed hard constraints to soft ones for jobs larger than\nhalf the original cell size, and allowed up to 0.2% tasks to go\npending if they were very “picky” and could only be placed\non a handful of machines; extensive experiments showed\nthat this produced repeatable results with low variance. If\nFigure 6: Segregating users would need more machines. The total\nnumber of cells and the additional machines that would be needed\nif users larger than the threshold shown were given their own\nprivate cells, for 5 different cells.\nwe needed a larger cell than the original we cloned the orig-\ninal cell a few times before compaction; if we needed more\ncells, we just cloned the original.\nEach experiment was repeated 11 times for each cell with\ndifferent random-number seeds. In the graphs, we use an er-\nror bar to display the min and max of the number of ma-\nchines needed, and select the 90%ile value as the “result” –\nthe mean or median would not reﬂect what a system admin-\nistrator would do if they wanted to be reasonably sure that\nthe workload would ﬁt. We believe cell compaction provides\na fair, consistent way to compare scheduling policies, and it\ntranslates directly into a cost/beneﬁt result: better policies\nrequire fewer machines to run the same workload.\nOur experiments focused on scheduling (packing) a\nworkload from a point in time, rather than replaying a long-\nterm workload trace. This was partly to avoid the difﬁculties\nof coping with open and closed queueing models [71, 79],\npartly because traditional time-to-completion metrics don’t\napply to our environment with its long-running services,\npartly to provide clean signals for making comparisons,\n\n\n=== PDF page 8 ===\n2\n4\n6\n8\n10\n-20\n0\n20\n40\n60\n80\nSub-cells\nOverhead [%]\ncell A\ncell B\ncell C\ncell D\ncell E\n(a) Additional machines that would be needed as a function of\nthe number of smaller cells for ﬁve different original cells.\n-50\n0\n50\n100\n150\n200\n250\n0\n20\n40\n60\n80\n100\nOverhead from partitioning [%]\nPercentage of cells\n2 subcells\n5 subcells\n10 subcells\n(b) A CDF of additional machines that would be needed to\ndivide each of 15 different cells into 2, 5 or 10 cells.\nFigure 7: Subdividing cells into smaller ones would require more machines. Figure 4\nshows how much smaller our real-world cells would be if\nwe were to apply cell compaction to them. The baselines in\nthe graphs that follow use these compacted sizes.\n5.2\nCell sharing\nNearly all of our machines run both prod and non-prod tasks\nat the same time: 98% of the machines in shared Borg cells,\n83% across the entire set of machines managed by Borg. (We\nhave a few dedicated cells for special uses.)\nSince many other organizations run user-facing and batch\njobs in separate clusters, we examined what would happen if\nwe did the same. Figure 5 shows that segregating prod and\nnon-prod work would need 20–30% more machines in the\nmedian cell to run our workload. That’s because prod jobs\nusually reserve resources to handle rare workload spikes, but\ndon’t use these resources most of the time. Borg reclaims the\nunused resources (§5.5) to run much of the non-prod work,\nso we need fewer machines overall.\nMost Borg cells are shared by thousands of users. Figure\n6 shows why. For this test, we split off a user’s workload\ninto a new cell if they consumed at least 10 TiB of mem-\nory (or 100 TiB). Our existing policy looks good: even with\nthe larger threshold, we would need 2–16× as many cells,\nand 20–150% additional machines. Once again, pooling re-\nsources signiﬁcantly reduces costs.\nBut perhaps packing unrelated users and job types onto\nthe same machines results in CPU interference, and so we\nwould need more machines to compensate? To assess this,\nwe looked at how the CPI (cycles per instruction) changed\nfor tasks in different environments running on the same ma-\nchine type with the same clock speed. Under these condi-\ntions, CPI values are comparable and can be used as a proxy\nfor performance interference, since a doubling of CPI dou-\nbles the runtime of a CPU-bound program. The data was\ngathered from ∼12000 randomly selected prod tasks over\na week, counting cycles and instructions over a 5 minute in-\nterval using the hardware proﬁling infrastructure described\nin [83], and weighting samples so that every second of CPU\ntime is counted equally. The results were not clear-cut.\n(1) We found that CPI was positively correlated with\ntwo measurements over the same time interval: the overall\nCPU usage on the machine, and (largely independently) the\nnumber of tasks on the machine; adding a task to a machine\nincreases the CPI of other tasks by 0.3% (using a linear\nmodel ﬁtted to the data); increasing machine CPU usage by\n10% increases CPI by less than 2%. But even though the\ncorrelations are statistically signiﬁcant, they only explain 5%\nof the variance we saw in CPI measurements; other factors\ndominate, such as inherent differences in applications and\nspeciﬁc interference patterns [24, 83].\n(2) Comparing the CPIs we sampled from shared cells to\nones from a few dedicated cells with less diverse applica-\ntions, we saw a mean CPI of 1.58 (σ = 0.35) in shared cells\nand a mean of 1.53 (σ = 0.32) in dedicated cells – i.e., CPU\nperformance is about 3% worse in shared cells.\n(3) To address the concern that applications in different\ncells might have different workloads, or even suffer selection\nbias (maybe programs that are more sensitive to interference\nhad been moved to dedicated cells), we looked at the CPI of\nthe Borglet, which runs on all the machines in both types of\ncell. We found it had a CPI of 1.20 (σ = 0.29) in dedicated\ncells and 1.43 (σ = 0.45) in shared ones, suggesting that\nit runs 1.19× as fast in a dedicated cell as in a shared\none, although this over-weights the effect of lightly loaded\nmachines, slightly biasing the result in favor of dedicated\ncells.\n\n\n=== PDF page 9 ===\n0\n20\n40\n60\n80\n100\n 0.01\n  0.1\n    1\n   10\n  100\n 1000\nPercentage of tasks\nRequested limit [cores, GiB, GiB/core]\nprod CPU\nnon-prod CPU\nprod memory\nnon-prod memory\nmemory-to-CPU-ratio\nFigure 8: No bucket sizes ﬁt most of the tasks well. We tested\nthe effects of the latter by partitioning the workload for a cell\nacross multiple smaller cells – by ﬁrst randomly permuting\nthe jobs and then assigning them in a round-robin manner\namong the partitions. (A core is a processor hyperthread,\nnormalized for performance across machine types.) Figure 8\nshows that they take advantage of this granularity: there are\nfew obvious “sweet spots” in the amount of memory or CPU\ncores requested, and few obvious correlations between these\nresources. These distributions are quite similar to the ones\npresented in [68], except that we see slightly larger memory\nrequests at the 90%ile and above.\nOffering a set of ﬁxed-size containers or virtual machines,\nalthough common among IaaS (infrastructure-as-a-service)\nproviders [7, 33], would not be a good match to our needs.\nTo show this, we “bucketed” CPU core and memory resource\nlimits for prod jobs and allocs (§2.4) by rounding them up to\nthe next nearest power of two in each resource dimension,\nstarting at 0.5 cores for CPU and 1 GiB for RAM. Figure 9\nshows that doing so would require 30–50% more resources\nin the median case. The upper bound comes from allocating\nan entire machine to large tasks that didn’t ﬁt after quadru-\n\n\n=== PDF page 10 ===\n0\n40\n80\n120\n160\ncapacity\nlimit\nreservation\nusage\n0\n40\n80\n120\n160\nMem\n0\n10k\n20k\nWeek 1 (baseline)\nWeek 2 (aggressive)\nWeek 3 (medium)\nWeek 4 (baseline)\nOOMs\n[%]\nCPU [%]\nFigure 12: More aggressive resource estimation can reclaim more resources, with little effect on out-of-memory events (OOMs).
